|---|---|
| `Scripts/Hud/HudTheme.cs` | Palette, the screen scale (`S` = height / 1080, 0.75-2), the shapes (`Rounded`, `Ring`, `Circle`, `Fill` = two-tone, `Body` = outline + shadow + fill, `Pill`, `Panel`, `Bar`, `Chip`, `KeyCap`), outlined text (`Text` / `TextPx`, shrinks to fit), the font choice, and `CachedText1` / `CachedText2` (a number's text is built once, not every frame). No textures: Unity draws the rounded rectangles itself (`GUI.DrawTexture` with a border radius). |
| `Scripts/Hud/HudAnim.cs` | The bounce: `Pop` (springs in; a widget that is only drawn while it applies pops again when it comes back), `Bump` (a punch when a value changes), `Count` (a figure runs to its target), `Pulse`, and the floaters. State is in small fixed arrays, nothing is allocated. |
| `Scripts/Hud/HudIcons.cs` | Coin, loot sack, backpack, siren, microphone, handcuffs, bullet, sparkle, status dot, badge: all drawn from rounded rectangles and circles. |
| `Scripts/Hud/HudWidgets.cs` | Every widget, each taking a small model struct or a few values (never the game's classes), so the same code draws the real HUD and a test gallery. |
| `Scripts/HeistHud.cs`, `BackupHud.cs`, `CustodyHud.cs` | Rewritten: read the game's numbers and call the widgets. |
| `Backpack.DrawHud`, `Guns/GunController.OnGUI`, `Net/Voice/VoiceChat.OnGUI`, `Interaction/Crosshair`, `Doors/DoorManager`, `Vendor`, `ElevatorPanel`, `ElevatorExtraction`, `TestMode` | Each calls the widgets for what it used to draw as plain text. |
| `Editor/NetDev/NetDevUi.cs` (+ `NetDevHarness.Ui`) | The picture scenario, see below. |

**The font**: a bundled `Resources/HudFont` (any .ttf the team drops there) wins; otherwise "Arial Rounded MT Bold" (ships with Microsoft Office; the owner's PC has it), then Trebuchet MS / Verdana / Arial in bold. A player without the rounded font still gets a clean bold face, but the look differs from machine to machine, so **adding a rounded TTF as `Resources/HudFont` is the way to make it identical everywhere** (the packs have none; ask the owner, do not download one).

**Cost** (measured in a Mono player with the profiler's "GC Allocated In Frame"; floors cost about the same): 0.9-1.4 ms of CPU per repaint and about 6 KB of garbage per frame. Shapes, clip groups and scaling make no garbage; every text draw or measurement in IMGUI makes about 44 bytes and the HUD has 100-140 text draws a frame, so the rest is IMGUI's own. The widths of texts and the strings of numbers are cached (that took it from 16 KB to 6 KB). If it has to go lower: bake the static labels (the key hints) to textures once, or draw only the figures that move.

**How it was tested**: (1) a throw-away Unity project (not in the repo) with a gallery scene that draws every widget with mock numbers over a stand-in backdrop, 17 states, including the cost numbers above; (2) the real game: `NetDevHarness.Ui` plays the Greybox scene and photographs the docks, a floor (with a cash floater), the pew's ammo, a pick-up prompt, quota met, a backup call and a pinned player; `[Ui] DONE fails=0`, no errors from the HUD classes. **IMGUI does not draw in batch mode** (neither an Editor nor a player started with `-batchmode` ever calls `OnGUI`), so a picture of it needs a real window. The agent ran the Editor on a **hidden Windows desktop** (`CreateDesktop`, then `CreateProcess` with `lpDesktop`), so nothing appeared on the owner's screen or took their focus; see 8.6.

**Not restyled yet** (still the plain look): the Tab inventory window (`Backpack.DrawInventory`, GUILayout), the shop window (`Vendor`), the elevator panel's screen, the hack / safe / intel screens (`Outpost/*`), the speech bubbles over guards and the dealer (`SpeechBubble`, `GuardBubbleHud`, `VendorChat`). The lobby menu and the Esc menu were restyled later the same evening (8.9, delivery 2). They can use the same kit. **Check first in Unity**: the cash and quota on the status card, the key caps along the bottom, a prompt over an item, the pew's ammo panel, being pinned (the plate, the number), and the night floors (the pale rim).

### 2.15 The guards' own elevator and the guard on duty (be48)

Added at the owner's request (2026-10-16): "add another elevator shaft that is strictly for the guards and it should be where backup comes from. The elevator should be aligned on each floor like a normal elevator shaft would be. Currently guards spawn in the outpost which can lead to problems if someone is in there when backup is alerted. Also the outpost should have one guard inside and after a player enters they have to knockout that guard before they get alerted or pinned. The guard inside the outpost should not see the players outside until they have the outpost door open and are inside the outpost". The owner answered the one question asked: the shaft goes in the **opposite corner** from the players' elevator. Built by two agents (a planner / generator patch by the "guards' elevator session", the tests and the apply by agent 3). Verified in Unity batch mode (below); **never seen by the assistants on a real screen** (the pictures of floors run, `Floors` 56 shots, show nothing wrong, but nobody looked at the shaft or the outpost closely).

**What changed for the player**
- Every office floor (and the boss floor) has a small walled car, the **guards' elevator**, in the corner cell opposite the service elevator: cell (columns - 1, rows - 1), so it stands in the same place on every floor, like a real shaft (the players' elevator is cell (0, 0)). Its one door faces the first open neighbour of its cell (west, south, east, north) and opens onto that cell's corridor; a lane is kept free of furniture from the door to the doorway of the cell. **All backup arrives in it** (`HeistManager.CallBackup` -> `FloorGenerator.SpawnReinforcements`: the guards appear inside the car, its door opens with a chime, they walk out). **Nothing arrives in the outpost any more.**
- The **guard outpost** (section 2.11) moved to the farthest cell that is not the shaft's; its elevator alcove is gone (the back wall now has lockers all along it; `OutpostInfo` has no `car` fields) and it holds **exactly one guard, "Guard_Outpost", on duty**: he stands at a post in the middle of the room facing the window (`GuardAI.stationary`, `StandGuard`, `postForward`), is **blind and deaf to everything outside the room** (`GuardAI.noticeGate`: a function that says whether a position is inside the room; `CanSee` and `OnNoise` ask it) and so notices a player only once the door is open **and** the player is inside. Then the ordinary rules run: he watches for `callSeconds` (2 s), then calls backup and chases / pins; **a hit in time stops it** (`WeaponHit` cuts the call; ten hits, or two pew bullets, put him down for good; a stunned guard does neither). A player who steps out of the room during the 2 s drops the call (the gate makes `StillSees` false); he walks to where he last saw them, looks round and goes back to his post (and faces the window again). A noise inside the room is heard (he goes to look), a noise outside is not, **the alarm siren excepted** (it is a siren, not a noise on the bus). He is unarmed unless the pew's floor rule arms him (`ArmedGuards`, 2.13). The patrols and the roaming backup keep out of the outpost.
- His cone on the floor (`GuardVision`) is clipped to the room's rectangle, so it does not draw a view out through the open door.

**Where it lives**: `Interior/FloorShape.cs` (`ShaftCell`, `FarthestCell(..., exceptC, exceptR)`, `PlanFor` puts `guestC/R` = the shaft and `outpostC/R` = the outpost cell into `PlanInput`), `Interior/OfficePlanner.cs` (`isGuest` / `isOutpost` are different cells; `PlanRing` for the car with its lane), `OfficePlanner.Outpost.cs` (no alcove, lockers along the back wall, `OutpostInfo.frontX/Z`), `OfficeLayout.cs`, `FloorGenerator.cs` (`BuildGuestElevator(guestCell)` is always built, `HasGuardShaft`, `GuardShaftCentre`, `GuardShaftOut`, `InGuardShaft`, `RandomWalkable` keeps roamers out of the car), `FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs` (`PlaceOutpostGuard`, `TryGetOutpostRoom`), `FloorGenerator.AfterHours.cs` and `FloorGenerator.Interior.cs` (patrol stops and ceiling panels), `GuardAI.cs` (`noticeGate`, `stationary`, `StandGuard`), `GuardVision.cs` (the clipped cone). The guard on duty is made last, with no random numbers, so mazes, loot and the other guards are what they were; he is a normal guard with a `NetId`, so clients see a puppet.

**Tests**: `NetDevTest.GuardShaftTest` (pure, in `PolicyTest`, 14 checks on 30 floors of five sizes: the shaft in the corner opposite the elevator and in the same cell on every floor of a size, the outpost elsewhere and the farthest, doors face open neighbours, one outpost, no alcove, nothing in the car or its lane or in front of the outpost's window, at least five lockers, deterministic), `NetDevHarness.Shaft` (`NetDevShaft.cs`, offline Play Mode, 78 checks: the shaft on floors 1, 3, 8, 12 and the boss floor, backup arrives in the car and walks out, none in the outpost, exactly one guard on duty, the gate for sight and hearing, the 2 s call, stepping out drops it, a hit cuts it, ten hits and no backup, left alone he calls and the backup comes from the car; one SKIP in a batch run: the cone on the floor needs a shader) and `NetDevHarness.ShaftNet` (two processes: the client has the same floor, the guard on duty as a puppet at his post, the shaft, the backup and the open door; `runmp.ps1 -Method ShaftNet -Roles host,c1`). `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` = "8" (a floor is built differently). The older scenarios give the known baseline failures (8.7); `Outpost` now has one more FAIL line, "hack: ...and opens again": that check only passed before because the backup officers, who used to appear inside the outpost, walked out through the locked door and left it open; it was already failing for the reason above it (the hack scenario's keys are never pressed in time - not so: 8.7 shows the real reason, the scenario went around the panel).

**Decisions made without asking** (change them if the owner objects): the guards just step out of closed doors with a chime (no moving car, no lit shaft); one guard on duty, unarmed unless the pew rule says so; he does not respawn; the alarm siren still sounds for everybody.

### 2.16 The two stairwell doors (be49)

Added at the owner's late request to the guards'-elevator session (2026-10-16): "There should also be a two doors one on each opposite side of the floor where backup guards can come from. This will prevent players from camping the guard elevator" / "These doors would represent stairwells". Built and applied by agent 3 after both original sessions hit the usage limit. **Backup no longer comes only out of the guards' elevator (2.15): it comes out of three doors.**

**What changed for the player**
- Every office floor has **two stairwells**, in the two corners that are neither elevator's: north-west = cell (0, rows - 1) and south-east = cell (columns - 1, 0), the same cells on every floor (so on opposite sides of the floor). Not on the boss floor or the docks. Each is a small walled landing (4.8 m outside, 4.2 m inside, walls 0.3 m) in the middle of its corner cell with a steel double door (2 m wide, `ElevatorDoor`, no chime) onto the corridor, the word **STAIRS** on a dark plate beside the door, three steps along the back wall and a red exit lamp inside. Nothing sends a player in there: the door is shut unless backup is coming (a player could walk in while it is open); it is scenery that the guards use.
- A backup call (`HeistManager.CallBackup` / `CallBackupAt` -> `FloorGenerator.SpawnReinforcements(target, n)`) chooses its doors with `OrderEntries`: the doors more than 12 m (`EntrySafeDistance`) from every **free** player first, in a random order, **the door the last call began with moved to the back**; then the doors that are closer, the farthest first. The guards of one call are given the doors one after the other (`order[i % order.Count]`), so two officers come out of two different doors when two are far enough. Standing at the guards' elevator makes the backup come out of the stairs; standing at a stairwell makes it come out of the elevator and the other stairwell. The chosen doors open (the elevator with its chime, a stairwell with a thud), the guards appear inside the landing and walk out, and each door shuts 7 s after its last use. If a floor has no door at all, the old `SpawnReinforcementsAnywhere` is the fallback.
- **Nothing else changed** for the outpost guard (2.15). The outpost is now the farthest cell that is none of the three door cells (`FloorShape.FarthestCellExcept`).

**Where it lives**: `Interior/FloorShape.cs` (`StairCells`, `FarthestCellExcept`; `PlanFor` fills `PlanInput.stairC/stairR/stairDirX/stairDirY` and puts the outpost in the farthest other cell), `Interior/OfficePlanner.cs` (`StairIndex`, `PlanStairRing`: a ring round a solid block of `StairHalf` = 2.4 m and a kept-clear lane 3.4 m wide from the landing to the doorway of the cell, like the guards' car), `FloorGenerator.cs` (`Stairwell` class, `BuildStairwells`, `BuildStairwell`, `BuildStairSign`, `StairCount` / `StairCentre(i)` / `StairOut(i)` / `StairDoorOpen(i)`, `GuardEntries`, `NearestFreePlayer`, `OrderEntries`, `SetEntryDoor`, `CloseEntryLater`, `lastEntry`, `InGuardShaft` also covers the landings so roamers keep out), `FloorGenerator.AfterHours.cs` (solid blocks and one ceiling panel per landing). **Replication**: the doors' state is one byte, `RunSync.vEntriesOpen` (`FloorGenerator.EntryDoorMask`: bit 0 the guards' elevator, bits 1 and 2 the stairwells; clients apply it with `NetEntryDoors`); the guards themselves replicate exactly as before (`BroadcastSpawnGuard` with the position). `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` = "9". Because the floor is built differently, old and new builds cannot share a run.

**Tests**: `NetDevTest.GuardShaftTest` has 7 more pure checks (two stairwells on every one of the 30 planned floors, in the two other corners, distinct from the elevators and from each other, the outpost in none of them, doors face open neighbours, nothing in a landing or in its lane); `NetDevHarness.Shaft` (92 checks) adds: the stairwells with their walls, door and sign on floors 1, 3, 8 and 12, the backup in the landing of one of the doors with that door open, all doors shut again after a call, camping at the elevator (both officers out of the two stairwells, the elevator door stays shut), camping at a stairwell (none out of it, two different doors), sixteen single-officer calls far from every door (never the same door twice running, all three used), no stairwells on the boss floor and the docks; `Shaft` with graphics also saves pictures `be_shot_stairs0_*`, `stairs1_*`, `guest_*` (closed, near, open); `ShaftNet` (two processes) checks that the client has the two stairwells and the same doors open as the host (the mask `3` = elevator and first stairwell in the run that was checked). The older scenarios gave the known failures of 8.7 (`Outpost` 19, `Doors` 2, `Sneak` 3 plus the native crash); all of them are explained and fixed there.

**Not seen by the owner yet**: look at a stairwell door in a run (the steel is very dark; the sign is a small dark plate), call backup near the elevator and watch the officers come out of the stairs, and check that a stairwell's corner cell still has furniture round it and nothing blocks the way to its door.

**Decisions made without asking** (change them if the owner objects): the stairwells are scenery (no stairs to climb, nothing to do inside); a distance of 12 m to the nearest free player decides which doors count as "away"; two guards spread over two doors; the doors shut 7 s after the last officer came out; a pinned or busted player does not count as someone to keep away from.

### 2.17 The floor-4 demo: armed guards, the pew in the outpost, the pew over the network, the demo ends at floor 4, a free pew (be50)

Added at the owner's request (2026-10-16 evening, to agent 1 after its first limit; that session was cut short by its usage limit with everything built and tested but **not applied**, and agent 2 (session `bad83f09`, resumed) finished and applied it): "Please add the armed guards on wave 4 and the pistol should be in the outpost on wave 4 100% of the time" / "For now please have it where after the 4th floor players cannot continue to the 5th floor basically making this a demo up to the 4th floor (the full game will remove this limitation)" / "Also after completion of the 4th floor the vendor should now sell the pew pistol for free so that players can test it out on the dummy in the loading docks". ("Wave 4" = floor 4.) Verified in Unity batch mode (below). **Never seen on a real screen by the assistants beyond the test pictures** (`NetDevHarness.DemoUi`: the shop before and after, floor 4, floor 4 complete, the docks with the demo over; `be_ui_demo_*.png`).

**What changed for the player**
- **Guards are armed from floor 4** (`ArmedGuards.DefaultFirstArmedFloor` = 4): every guard made on floors 4-13 - patrols, the backup that comes out of the three doors (2.15, 2.16) and the guard on duty in the outpost; floors 1-3 stay unarmed. `GuardVisual` adds the `GuardGun` when a guard is made (the F1 menu can still arm everybody or move the floor). Armed guards behave as in 2.13.
- **The pew lies in the outpost of floor 4, every run** (`OutpostLoot.PewFloor` = 4): `OutpostLoot.Roll` puts `PewKind` (3) first in the stash's weapons, so it is the first weapon put on a desk, and if the stash is over `MaxItems` (6) a valuable is dropped before it ever is; the melee weapons are rolled as before; no other floor's outpost has one. `FloorGenerator.FillOutpost` makes it with `RangedWeapon.Create` (given a yaw: it takes no random number, so the floor is not disturbed). To get it the team hacks the outpost (2.11) and knocks out the guard on duty (2.15), who is armed on floor 4.
- **The pew works over the network** (`Net/RunSync.Guns.cs`, new, and `GunController`): the shooter's machine shows its own flash, tracer and sound at once and sends the host the muzzle, the direction and the guard it had in its sights (`RequestGunShot` -> `GunShotRequestRpc`); **the host traces the bullet itself** (`Ballistics.Trace`), decides what it hit (a guard through `WeaponHitHost` with the pew's bullet weight, the training dummy loses 60, a loose item is shoved), makes the 45 m noise and announces where the bullet ended (`PlayerShotRpc`: slot, end point, result 0 flew off / 1 wall or desk / 2 guard / 3 guard down for good / 4 dummy); the other machines show the shot in the shooter's hand (`GunController.ShowRemoteShot`, `PoseRemote`: a teammate's pew is in his hand, the arm raised, on every machine). The host checks the claim: a pew in his hand (`Backpack.Equipped`), not in custody, the muzzle within 4 m of the player, not faster than 70 % of the fire interval; a guard the shooter saw but the host's trace just missed (he has moved on) is hit all the same when he is within 1 m of the shooter's line and nothing solid is between (`Forgiven`). `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` = "10".
- **The demo ends at floor 4** (`TowerRules.DemoLastFloor` = 4): the service elevator goes no higher than floor 4 (`TowerRules.UpFrom` gives -1 for a floor above it, from floor 4 and from the docks), nothing builds floor 5 or higher (`TowerManager.RideElevator` / `RideTo` refuse it), the panel says why ("The demo ends at Floor 4: the rest of the tower is in the full game"; "Finished floors are closed" in the docks once the demo is over), the HUD counts "FLOOR n/4" while the tower is a demo (`TowerRules.DemoStops`), and the banner after floor 4's quota says "That was the last floor of the demo: return to the Loading Docks". The test menu's `TowerManager.GoToLevel` is **not** stopped (developers can still go anywhere with F1). **To remove the limit for the full game set `TowerRules.DemoLastFloor` to `TowerRules.BossFloor` (13)** - `NetDevDemoTest` checks that a limit of 13 brings the old rules back - and look at `Vendor.FreePewAfterFloor` (below) and the two banner texts in `HeistHud`.
- **Back in the docks with floor 4 finished** (the elevator would resume at floor 5: `TowerRules.DemoOver(ResumeLevel)`): the banner says **DEMO COMPLETE** ("The vendor has a pew pistol for free: try it on the training dummy") and so does the status card's bar. **The vendor sells the pew for nothing** (`Vendor.PewForSale(resume)`: `resume > Vendor.FreePewAfterFloor` = 4): the eighth and last entry of the catalog (`Vendor.PewIndex` = 7, "Pew Pistol", shown as FREE, 2.6 lbs); it is not listed before the demo is complete and the host refuses a purchase before then ("The dealer has nothing like that for sale yet"); one pew each ("You already have a pew"); it goes into the pack and the hand with a full magazine, and the training dummy in the docks takes 60 per bullet (two bring it down). Bought in a networked run the host makes it and every machine gets the same item (`BroadcastSpawnItem` with the catalog index; `Vendor.SpawnAt` makes a gun for `isGun`).

**Where it lives**: `Guns/ArmedGuards.cs` and `GunRules.cs` (`DefaultFirstArmedFloor`), `Outpost/OutpostLoot.cs` (`PewFloor`, `PewKind`, `Roll`, `WeaponName`), `FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs` (`FillOutpost`), `Guns/GunController.cs` (firing when `NetRole.Synced`, `ShowRemoteShot`, `PoseRemote`, `ShotsSeen`), `Net/RunSync.Guns.cs`, `Net/NetConst.cs` ("10"), `TowerRules.cs` (`DemoLastFloor`, `Beyond`, `IsDemoEnd`, `DemoStops`, `DemoOver`, `UpFrom`), `TowerManager.cs`, `ElevatorExtraction.cs` (the panel's reasons), `HeistHud.cs` and `Hud/HudWidgets.cs` (`StatusModel.demoDone`, the banners), `Vendor.cs` (`Product.isGun`, `PewForSale`, `FreePewAfterFloor`, `PewIndex`).

**Tests** (dev tree, run on the final code 2026-10-16 20:04-20:14, and the compile + pure part again just before applying): pure `NetDevTest.PewLootTest` (11 checks: armed from floor 4, exactly one pew in floor 4's outpost and none elsewhere, the first weapon, deterministic, a place for its note) and `DemoRulesTest` (18 checks: the tower ends at 4, floors 1 and 2 lead up only when their quota is met, from the docks the elevator resumes at 1-4, no way up from floor 4, nothing above 4 is ever a destination, a limit of 13 brings the old rules back, the HUD counts 5 stops, the panel's reasons, the vendor entry, when it is for sale, its weight) - **234 pure PASS in all**; `NetDevHarness.PewLoot` (offline, floors 1-6, 26 checks), `Demo` (offline: the elevator refuses floor 5, the demo is over, the pew is for sale and free, in the pack and the hand with a full magazine, a second one is refused, a bullet takes 60 off the dummy and two bring it down; 34 checks), `GunNet` (two processes: the client's shot reaches the host, the host's reaches the client, a teammate's gun is drawn and fires on every machine; host 9 + client 8), `DemoNet` (two processes: the demo limit and the free pew on both machines; host 8 + client 5), `Pew` 69, `Armed` (host + client), `Shaft` 92, `ShaftNet`, `Offline` and `Floors` (56 pictures) all with 0 FAIL; `Doors` 2, `Outpost` 19 and `Sneak` 3 gave the known baseline of 8.7 and nothing new (all three are clean since the night of 2026-10-16: 8.7). Run the two-process ones with `runmp.ps1 -Method GunNet|DemoNet -Roles host,c1`, the pictures with `NetDevHarness.DemoUi` (graphics, hidden desktop, 8.6).

**Check first in Unity** (nobody on the team has played it): (1) start a run and climb to floor 4: the guards carry a pew, the guard on duty in the outpost too, and so does the backup that comes out of the elevator and the stairs; (2) hack floor 4's outpost: a pew lies on one of its desks; take it, shoot a guard from a distance (he goes down in two hits; the crosshair turns red over a guard); with a friend: the friend sees and hears your shots; (3) finish floor 4 (quota met): the banner says it was the last floor; ride to the docks: DEMO COMPLETE, the shop lists "Pew Pistol FREE", buy it, shoot the training dummy; (4) the elevator panel's floors 5-13 stay dark and pressing one says why; (5) in a networked run both machines see the same shop entry and the same demo state.

**Done afterwards (2.18)**: the full Q/A pass of floors 1-4 and the Windows player build for the friends' demo (the owner asked for them first, then said "Wait that is wrong" and asked for this implementation instead, then asked for them again). **Not done**: `HowToPlayContent.Controls` still lists "1 - 7  Buy from the vendor's shop" although the shop is clicked with the mouse.

**Decisions made without asking** (change them if the owner objects): "wave 4" means floor 4; every guard from floor 4 up is armed (not only some); the demo's end is the docks after floor 4 (the team can go back down and replay floors 1-4, the finished ones are closed); one free pew per player, for sale only after the demo is complete; the test menu is not limited by the demo.

### 2.18 The friends' demo: the full Q/A of floors 1-4 and the first Windows build (be51)

The owner's request (2026-10-16 night, after the floor-4 demo of 2.17 was applied): "Yes: do a full Q/A of floors 1 to 4 in the dev copy and, once it passes, build the game for the friends' demo." Done by agent 2 (session `bad83f09`). **The game's rules did not change**; two small fixes came out of the Q/A (below). **The build is in the repo folder `Builds\Demo_2026-10-16\` (git-ignored, but OneDrive syncs it): `OvertimeEscape_Demo_2026-10-16.zip` (39.1 MB, SHA-256 `16F1E5F4C3937236034FCF233A3B7A1813BCB7A29BAFB41CE5F0C78E1DEE6458`; THIS is what goes to the friends), the same build unzipped (`OvertimeEscape_Demo_2026-10-16\OvertimeEscape.exe`, with the friends' `README.txt` and `steam_appid.txt` = 480 / Spacewar) and `QA_results\` (the Q/A summary, three autopilot reports, 11 pictures).** Everybody must run exactly this build (`NetConst.ProtocolVersion` "10"). **The run-start cinematic (2.19) is NOT in this first build: the second build, with it, is described at the end of this section (`Builds\Demo_2026-10-16_intro\`, protocol "11").**

**Results** (final code, 2026-10-16 22:08-22:12; the tested scratch tree `BE_qa` equals the repo by SHA-256, `cmp_trees.ps1`): compile 0 errors; pure tests 234 PASS. Offline Play Mode: `LootQa` (new; 8 seeds x floors 1-4: loot is 8-13x the quota, every piece carriable by one player, reachable on foot from a patrolling guard's position (nooks <= max(3, 2 %)), guards counted / on the NavMesh / armed by the floor-4 rule, exactly one `Guard_Outpost`, the guards' shaft and 2 stairwells, the pew only in floor 4's outpost; then a real banking loop: loot into the car, FloorComplete, ride to the docks, surplus becomes cash, DemoOver) 301 PASS; `Demo`, `Offline`, `PewLoot`, `Pew`, `Shaft`, `Floors` PASS. Two processes (host + client over the loopback): `Armed`, `GunNet`, `DemoNet`, `ShaftNet`, `MeleeNet` (new: the client buys a crowbar at the counter, the team rides to floor 1, the client knocks a patrolling guard down, a second hit counts), `OutpostNet` (8.7): 0 FAIL. Five players (`Qa`, `BE_PLAYERS=5`, 117 s): 0 FAIL after three stale expectations in `NetDevQa.cs` (a backup door is the guards' elevator or a stairwell; the demo's hidden elevator does not go to floor 13, so the host's test hook does; the guard walks on, so the swing is retried). **The BUILT player played itself** (`QaAutopilot`, new): solo - menu, docks, floors 1-4 (guards' elevator, outpost, loot, the pew on floor 4), floor complete, back to the docks, DEMO COMPLETE and the free pew for sale: **48 PASS, 0 FAIL, 0 errors or exceptions logged, 86 s, no magenta pixel in any of its pictures** (a stripped shader or an error shows as magenta); two built players as host + client over the loopback: **host 9 PASS, client 10 PASS** (both see both players, the client's guards are puppets, the quota is the host's, floors 1-2). Speed (RTX 3060 Ti, 1920 x 1080, Ultra): about 230 fps in the docks and with the guards about; 60-100 fps in the first 3 s after a floor loads; two players on one machine about 40 fps each (two windows on one GPU); managed memory under 25 MB.

**Fixes found by the Q/A:** (1) `Net/BootLoader.cs`: `Application.runInBackground = true` before the first scene loads. The project setting was OFF: a host who alt-tabs (chat, Steam) would have frozen the whole team and cut the voice (the built player's log says `runInBackground True`). (2) `Net/Voice/VoiceCodec.cs` (`SteamVoiceInput.SetActive`): nothing is stopped once Steam is shut down; `SteamService.OnApplicationQuit` shuts Steam down before `VoiceChat.OnDestroy`, so a host who had talked logged `InvalidOperationException: Steamworks is not initialized` at every quit (harmless noise in the friends' logs). (3) Test-only: the three `NetDevQa.cs` lines above and the autopilot's client wait (a client's `NetSession.State` stays `InLobby`; only the host's turns `InRun`, so a client waits for the docks).

**How to make the build again and re-run the Q/A** (reference copies of the scripts are in the repo's `DevTools\QaScripts` (see its README); the originals are in session A's scratchpad `C:\Users\phick\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-phick-Eternum\bad83f09-2f5c-45af-91fd-630f53fb6650\scratchpad`: `qa_final.ps1` does everything below in one go on the scratch tree `BE_qa`: tooling, compile + pure tests, build, package, play the unzipped build solo and as host + client, summary `qa_summary_<tag>.txt`; also `qa_regress.ps1`, `qa_mp.ps1`, `qa_new_apply.ps1`, `cmp_trees.ps1`, `hd.ps1`).

Leave the `*_BurstDebugInformation_DoNotShip` folder out of the zip.

Without `-be-autopilot` `QaAutopilot` does nothing.
- Editor scenarios (scratch copy): `NetDevHarness.LootQa` (offline), `MeleeNet` (two processes), and those of 2.17 and 8.6.

### 2.19 The run-start boss cinematic ("the Intro") - the owner's request of 2026-10-16 night - STATUS: DONE: applied to the repo (2026-10-16 23:08) and built into the second friends' demo (2.18)

**UPDATE 2026-10-17 (be55): the cutscene was redone** - the workers (Tiny Toon people) watch the boss's monologue on a TV on the Loading Docks' wall and one of them says the owner's own line afterwards; the office, the monologue, the skip and the vote below are unchanged but the picture is now a TV in the docks: see 2.21 (7) and (9) and `Intro/DockTv.cs`.

**The owner's words, in order (sent while the Q/A ran):** (1) "If possible are you able to make a cinematic to be shown every time a new run is started (it can be skipped by holding space)? It should be showing the boss saying that he is taking away the loading dock workers bonuses this year so that he can buy a private jet". (2) "Actually the boss' office isn't built yet so the cinematic doesn't really work". (3) "This is a reference image" (a picture, described below). (4) "If in multiplayer 50%+ need to hold skip to skip the "Intro"(which is the cinematic)" and "50%+ of the party members I mean". (5) "After finishing the build please work on the cinematic right away". And: "When our limit is almost hit please catch up the design notes and memory and leave a detailed layout of our plan, what we got done and where we are headed so another developer and agent can pickup where we leave off please" (this section, 8.10 and the memory notes are that).

**Reading of it (decisions made without asking; change them if the owner objects):** (a) the office does not exist as a place yet, so the cinematic brings its own set: a small, reusable **boss office** built from code and from the packs the project already has (`BossOffice.Build`; floor 13's real boss office can reuse it later), looking like the reference picture; (b) "every time a new run is started" = every START RUN from the lobby, played on every machine as soon as its Loading Docks are built (NOT when the team merely comes back to the docks after a floor); (c) skipping = HOLD SPACE for 1 s; **in multiplayer the Intro is skipped for everybody as soon as at least 50 % of the party members are holding it (holders x 2 >= party size: 1 of 1, 1 of 2, 2 of 3, 2 of 4, 3 of 5)**, with a "SKIP n/m" readout; the host counts the holders and announces the skip (`Net/RunSync.Intro.cs`: a hold-state `ServerRpc` and a skip `ClientRpc`, so `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` becomes "11"); (d) the Intro is off in the Editor's batch tests (`Application.isBatchMode`, so every existing scenario runs untouched) and the built player's autopilot skips it unless it is testing it; (e) the owner writes the game's text: the boss's lines are PLACEHOLDERS written to the owner's brief and kept in one list (`IntroContent`) for the owner to rewrite.

**The reference picture** (a film still the owner sent: the look and the layout, not to be copied): a man in a dark blue shirt and a loosened dark tie sits behind a wide **glass-topped desk** in a tall **black ladder-back leather chair** (a slatted back); behind him **floor-to-ceiling windows with thin dark mullions** show a vast city and horizon far below under a bright cloudy sky; at the left a **Tiffany-style table lamp** and stacked leather document boxes; at the right a big black **flat monitor** seen from the side; on the desk papers, a folder and **small metal triangle / pyramid ornaments**; a wide cinematic frame (about 2.3 : 1), a medium shot from across the desk, the boss talking to the camera (to the workers).

**CURRENT STATE (2026-10-16 23:19) - read this first.** The Intro is DONE: written, looked at, tested in the built player and **APPLIED to the repo's `Assets` at 23:08** (the five shared files patched with backups in agent 4's scratchpad `real_backup_before_intro`, 13 new files with their `.meta`; the real project and the tested tree `BE_qa` were compared by content afterwards: 437 files, 0 differences). It is in the second friends' build (2.18, protocol "11"). The staging folder `DevTools\IntroCinematic_pending` was removed (everything in it is in `Assets`, newer, or in `DevTools\QaScripts`).
- **The offline path (the owner's report, 23:46; DONE):** "Currently in play mode the cinematic does not play when starting a match", then "Even in the intro build I don't see the cinematic playing". The first version armed the director only from `RunSync.OnNetworkSpawn`, i.e. only in a NETWORKED run (HOST LOCAL or Steam, then START RUN). The menu's **PLAY SOLO** (`SceneManager.LoadScene(NetConst.RunScene)`) and **Play pressed on the Greybox scene in the Editor** are OFFLINE: no `NetworkManager` session, no `RunSync`, so nothing armed it. The tests had missed it: they started runs through a lobby of one (`introsolo`), and the plain autopilot's solo run is offline but switches the Intro off. **Reproduced first** (built player, new mode `introoffline`: `FAIL timeout (60 s) waiting for the Intro to start after PLAY SOLO`), **then fixed:** `IntroCinematic.WatchScenes` (a `RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod(AfterSceneLoad)` plus the `SceneManager.sceneLoaded` event) calls `ArmOffline` when the run scene loads with `!NetSession.Online`; that director waits for the docks as before (`LocalReady`: the player is `WobblyPlayerRegistry.All.Count > 0`, not a `NetPlayer`), then runs on `Time.unscaledTimeAsDouble` (0.6 s lead-in, `OfflineLeadIn`), one player, a held Space for a second skips it (`UpdateOffline`). The offline Restart button (`HeistHud.DrawEndButton` reloads the scene) is a new run, so it plays again. **Tested:** the built player (`introoffline`: played out 7 PASS, skipped 6 PASS, on the unzipped final build too) and the REAL Editor's Play Mode on the hidden desktop (`ui_run.ps1`: `NetDevHarness.IntroOffline` = the Greybox scene played directly, `IntroSolo` = Boot, menu, the PLAY SOLO load: both 0 FAIL, pictures looked at); the batch scenarios (Offline, Demo, Pew) and a non-batch picture scenario (Menus) are unchanged. Every OTHER non-batch Editor scenario switches the cinematic off (`NetDevHarness` sets `IntroCinematic.SkipForTests = !mode.StartsWith("intro")` when Play Mode is entered), batch runs never play it (`Enabled`). **Lesson: an offline run has no `RunSync` - anything armed from it must also be armed offline, and a run-start feature needs a test for BOTH ways in (lobby run and PLAY SOLO).**
- **What it looks like now** (every beat was looked at twice with `qa_shots.ps1`: pictures `s0a` .. `s6b` in agent 4's scratchpad): a 2.39:1 picture with black letterbox bars. The boss (a Tiny Toon person, `IntroContent.BossModelId` = `male_b`: brown hair, brown jacket, striped shirt, and a red tie made of two boxes in `BossOffice`) sits in the tall black ladder-back chair behind a glass-topped desk (dark glass; a front panel hides his legs) with the stained-glass Tiffany lamp and leather boxes at the left, a big black monitor at the right, papers, a folder and three silver pyramids. Floor-to-ceiling windows with thin mullions (every 1.5 m) look out over a city of about 2500 towers (real geometry, faded into haze with distance, a window texture on the near ones) under a cloudy sky with a soft sun; at 18.4 s a private jet (white, gold stripe and fin) flies across the windows and the camera follows it. Seven beats, 27 s (`IntroRules.Beats`): wide (2 s, no words), medium, medium close, desk, medium, window + jet, low. The subtitles ("THE BOSS" and the line typed out) and the "HOLD SPACE TO SKIP" meter sit INSIDE the letterbox bars (when a bar is at least 12.5 % of the screen height; on a screen so wide that it has no bars they lie over the picture on a dark plate).
- **How the 22:40 problems were fixed**: (a) the Tiny Toon model's origin is the middle of its 2 m capsule (its feet are 1 m lower) and its head is huge (with the host at y 0.92 the eyes are at about y 1.2, the chin at 0.9, the hair at 1.78): `BossOffice.BossSeat` = (0, 0.92, 1.3) (he was at y 0.55, so only his cap and eyes showed above the desk); the camera rigs aim at his eyes (`IntroCinematic.Rigs`, look y about 1.2 to 1.3). (b) The flat skyline picture (with its moire arcs) is gone: the city is real geometry in three meshes (near, middle with faint windows, far without). `Sprites/Default` writes no depth, so every mesh holds its quads far rows first (a painter's order), each corner is faded towards `BossOffice.Haze` by its own distance (aerial perspective), and the meshes carry explicit sorting orders (-7, -8, -9; the sky -10) so they are drawn before anything else that is transparent (the lamp's glass); the sky is one big quad (2048 x 512, painted once: clouds and a soft sun). (c) Mullions are 0.07 m wide. (d) `male_b` and a tie instead of the cap (the tie must stand 0.34 m in front of the boss's origin: at 0.22 it was hidden inside his shirt). (e) All six shots were re-aimed; the Window shot's camera sits 2.5 m from the glass and swings across the jet's path (`BossOffice.JetPoint`).
- **Tools**: `QaAutopilot` mode `-be-autopilot-net introshots` (a picture at 35 % and 92 % of every beat; `-be-intro-model <id>` tries another boss through `BossOffice.ModelOverride`), `qa_shots.ps1` (builds the player and runs it) and `qa_intro.ps1` (the four built-player tests), both in `DevTools\QaScripts`.

### 2.20 Proximity voice: players hear each other only from a distance (be54)

**The owner's words (2026-10-17):** "Please make it so players can only hear other players VOIP from a certain distance, later on walkie talkies will help cover distancer but they will be purchased at the vendor", then "And produce the build please", "Right now VOIP works from wherever" and "Like you can hear people across the floor".

**The bug behind it (measured, not guessed):** the old code called itself proximity voice (a 3D `AudioSource` with a linear falloff from 2.5 to 30 m) but a voice was heard at FULL volume from anywhere. The two-process test `NetDevHarness.VoiceRange` (a client stands 3 to 70 m from a talking host and reads the loudness of the final mix with `AudioListener.GetOutputData`; this works in batch mode) gave 0.304, 0.284 and 0.284 at 4, 11 and 21 m, with the source reporting `spatialBlend 1` and `volume 1`: **the engine's 3D falloff and panning never reach audio that is written inside `OnAudioFilterRead`**, which is how `VoiceVoice` plays the voice stream (a looping one-sample clip is only the carrier). So the voice had neither a distance nor a direction.

**What it does now** (`Scripts/Net/Voice/VoiceRules.cs` pure rules, `VoiceVoice.cs` the playback, `VoiceChat.cs` the wiring): (1) the volume by distance is applied to the samples themselves (`VoiceRules.Gain`: 1 up to 4 m, then `(1 - t)^2` down to 0 at 20 m, `t` being how far between the two; half way it is a quarter: measured 56 % at 8 m, 20 % at 13 m, 7 % at 16 m, nothing from about 18 m), the engine's 3D is switched off for these sources; (2) the voice comes from the side the speaker is on (`PanFor`, `LeftGain` / `RightGain`: only the ear away from the voice gets quieter, down to 30 %); (3) the HOST sends a voice packet to a player only when he is within 24 m of the speaker (the range plus a 4 m margin so a voice fades out instead of being cut: `VoiceRules.ShouldRelay`), so a voice out of earshot costs no bandwidth and cannot be received at all; every machine also skips decoding a voice it cannot hear (`VoiceChat.Play`), and the "Player N is talking" pill therefore only shows speakers you can hear; (4) the lobby has no bodies, so there everybody still hears everybody (0.8, as before); in a run a voice whose speaker has no body is not heard. Guards still hear you by how loud you are (3 to 30 m, `VoiceNoise`): unchanged.

**Walkie-talkies (not built; the hook is):** `VoiceChat.RadioOf` (a `Func<ulong, bool>`: does this client carry a radio?) is the one thing to set. Two players who both carry one are heard at any distance (`VoiceRules.RouteFor` gives `Route.Radio`, gain 0.85, in the middle); the host's relay uses the same rule; beside each other the air still wins (full volume). Until a walkie-talkie exists nobody does. To build it: a vendor item (`Vendor.Catalog`), a `NetworkVariable<bool>` on `NetPlayer` that is true while the item is in the pack, and `VoiceChat.RadioOf = id => (the NetPlayer of that client).HasRadio` - every machine needs the flag of every player, because the listener decides too. Probably also a radio sound (a band-pass in `VoiceVoice`) and a key to talk over it. The pure rules and the two-process test already cover the radio route (the test sets the hook on both machines and the voice is heard at 70 m).

**Tuning:** the numbers are constants at the top of `VoiceRules.cs`: `FullVolumeDistance` 4, `MaxDistance` 20, `RelayMargin` 4, `PanDepth` 0.7, `RadioGain` 0.85, `LobbyGain` 0.8. An office cell of a floor is 18 m and a floor is 8 x 5 cells (144 x 90 m), so 20 m is about one cell: people in the same or the next room hear each other, people across the floor do not. The owner did not name a distance; if it feels too short or too long, change `MaxDistance` (and `FullVolumeDistance` with it) and nothing else.

**Tested:** 15 new pure checks (`NetDevVoiceRulesTest.cs`, 262 pure PASS in all); the two-process `VoiceRange` (`qa_mp.ps1 -Runs 'VoiceRange:host,c1'`; host + client on floor 4, eight distances from 3 to 70 m, a 0.4 tone for the microphone): the voice's peak follows the rules at every distance (1.00 / 0.56 / 0.20 / 0.07 of full, then silence), nothing at all from 22 m on (final mix included) and not one packet beyond 24 m (the host held back 405 of 1282), the louder ear is the one on the speaker's side (both sides), and with the radio hook set on both machines the voice is heard at 70 m: 17 client and 2 host checks, 0 FAIL (the same test on the old code showed no falloff at all). The built game still plays itself (solo 48, host 9, client 10 PASS) and the six cinematic tests pass on it. Five players `Qa` (voice between five, everybody standing in the elevator, so within full volume): the whole 5-player suite passed with the new voice (host 46, c1 45, c2 33, c3 44, c4 43 and the late joiner 5 checks, 0 FAIL): every machine hears its four teammates, the host relayed 3228 packets. **Not tested:** real Steam voice with a real microphone (a tone stands in for it; the Steam decoder path is unchanged), five players spread over a big floor, hearing through walls (distance only: no muffling, a wall does not make a voice quieter), and how it SOUNDS (nobody has listened: the numbers come from the measurements above).

Decoration never stops a floor (a failure is a warning).
3. **No way back to the basement before the quota is met**: `TowerRules.DownFrom(level, floorComplete)` (-1 until `HeistPhase.FloorComplete`), used by `ElevatorPanel` and `ElevatorExtraction` (`RefreshPanel` / `HostPress` / `WhyNot` say why) - the host decides, so a client cannot go down either (the five-player `Qa` checks it on every machine). Unchanged: nothing goes down from the docks or the boss floor, and once the quota is met the way down is open as before (the demo end of 2.17 included).
4. **The loot is small valuables, not furniture**: `Interior/Valuables.cs` (a catalog of 13 kinds: Smartphone, Gold Watch, Cash Envelope, Wallet, Gold Pen, Headphones, Camera, Tablet, Cash Stack, Handheld Console, Laptop, Casino Chips, Jewelry Box - value, weight, footprint, how often, which room themes like them), `Interior/ValuablePlan.cs` (where: free spots on top of desks, tables and counters, at most two per surface, spread over as many rooms as possible, never more than `LootPlan.CellCap` in a cell, never in the guard outpost), `FloorGenerator.Valuables.cs` (`PlaceValuables` / `BuildValuable`: each is made of a few primitives, is a pack item (E), is thrown with the left mouse button, is banked by its value in the elevator and never breaks). 64 + 3 x floor of them per floor (67 on floor 1, 76 on floor 4, 100 on floor 12), worth in step with the quota (LootQa still wants 8 to 13 times the quota), plus the few big treasures (`LootTable`: stapler, espresso machine, server, trophy, safe...), fewer and roomier than before (11 on floor 1, 14 on floor 4). **No piece of furniture is loot any more** (monitors, printers, copiers, coolers... are decoration); `NetDevEtr` checks it on floors 1-4.
5. **Carrying together** (`HeavyLoot.cs`, new `LootShards.cs`): what was wrong was the piece's own carriers hurting it (it bumped the people holding it and whatever the pair walked into, at speeds of their own making, and a few knocks shattered it silently - the "disappeared" piece). Now a held piece (or one set down a moment ago) takes no damage, a person bumping it never hurts it, one knock never does more than `MaxHit` (45 of 100), it is not solid to the people who hold it, turns slowly and never goes faster than `MaxCarrySpeed` (9 m/s); a stuck piece (more than 3.2 m from the carriers' hands for 0.8 s) is brought to them, one that falls out of the world is put back where it last stood safe, and when a piece does shatter everybody sees a burst of shards, hears a crash and reads the banner ("ESPRESSO MACHINE SHATTERED - $1700 lost"; "... fell out of the world - put back where it last stood"). Fewer pieces need two people and they are placed where two carriers fit.
6. **Hold Z to scan the room you stand in** (`LootScanner.cs` - added to the `HeistManager` object in `Awake` - and `LootScanRules.cs`): a pulse leaves the player and races over the floor of THAT room (the owner: "only work within the room and show items in that room"), marks every valuable lying in it (a diamond and a label with what, how much and how far, seen through furniture), the top of the screen names the room and counts what is in it ("SCANNING ROOM 111A   2 valuables here, about $1,745"; "SCANNING THE HALLWAY   nothing to steal here"); marks fade six seconds after the last pulse, another pulse leaves every couple of seconds while the key is held, moving to another room drops the old room's marks at once, something taken loses its mark. Purely local (every machine has the same loot in the same places). Nothing is scanned at the docks, in the elevator car, with a screen open, while pinned or busted, or during the cutscene. It is listed on the Keybinds screen.
7. **The cutscene is in the Loading Docks** (the monologue of 2.19 now plays on a TV): `Intro/DockTv.cs` hangs a flat screen on the docks' south wall 3 m from the east wall (`FloorGenerator.DockTvPoint`, with a clear space of storage in front of it), the boss's office is filmed into its screen, and a second camera in the docks films the TV and the workers in ten beats (35 s: the monologue 27 s, static, the workers' line, out); the players' own bodies are hidden meanwhile; the workers are stand-ins in a row 3.4 m from the wall, one per player (a lone player gets a **COWORKER** beside them), who all face the TV; after the static one of them - picked from the run's seed, so every machine agrees, and always the coworker when there is one - turns to another and says **"Hey call that guy you were talking about."** (the owner's own words, `IntroContent.FinalLine`; the subtitle names the speaker, PLAYER n or COWORKER) and the other turns back. The skip (hold Space, half of the party in multiplayer), the start on both paths (lobby run and PLAY SOLO) and the letterbox are unchanged (2.19).
8. **SECURITY OFFICE signs**: `DirectionSigns` / `FloorGenerator.Signs.cs`: a dark red plate with salmon lettering and an arrow (the same look and rule as the room-number signs: the arrow points the way a shortest walk through the doorways to the outpost begins); 3 to 6 per floor (5 on an 8 x 5 floor), the first in the cell nearest the elevator, the rest far from each other so one is always near; the outpost's own cell has none. All the signs of a floor are still ONE mesh (now six materials).
9. **The cutscene's characters are Tiny Toon people** (the owner: "the toon characters not the colorful ones"): `IntroCinematic.ToonOf` / `PickToon`: a player who chose a Tiny Toon person in Customize is shown as them; everybody else (the default is a colourful Party character) is shown as a Tiny Toon person by slot (male_a, female, male_b, then round again), the coworker as another one than the player; the boss is `male_b` (`IntroContent.BossModelId`). A toon person has one face, so the workers' mood is body language now (`IntroCinematic.Pose`: head and chest drop when the boss takes the bonuses, lift and shake a little when it is over) instead of the Party face swap, which is still used if a machine has no toon people. The players themselves are still the colourful Party characters in the game: only the cutscene was asked for.

7. **Glass fronts on some offices** (`FloorGenerator.Glass.cs`, the owner's "see through glass walls ... I mean glass windows"): the wall of an office / meeting room / training room that looks into a corridor gets a long glass window (solid piers, sill 0.9 m, head 2.45 m, transom bar, steel mullions every 1.35 m, faint bluish panes); offices at the edge of the building nearly always have one and theirs reaches from 0.1 m to 3.05 m; inner offices now and then (about 20 per floor). **Their doors are glass too** (the owner: "the offices with glass walls should also have glass doors": `DressGlassDoor`, `MakeGlassLeafMesh`: both leaves are frames with one big opening and a faint pane on both faces; the door's blocker collider is unchanged). Nothing hangs on a windowed wall (no mounted picture or board, no direction sign). Visual only: the wall's collider stays, so walking, shooting and what the guards see do not change (a glass wall guards could see through would be a gameplay change: not made). The four real corners of a floor are the lifts and stairs, so "corner" here means the offices on the building's edge, and any room with two outside sides.
8. **The pew first lies in floor 3's outpost and is sold after floor 3** (`OutpostLoot.PewFloor` = 3, `Vendor.FreePewAfterFloor` = 3, the vendor's blurb "Free after floor 3"); the guards are armed from floor 4 as before (`ArmedGuards.DefaultFirstArmedFloor` = 4), so the players have the gun a floor before the first armed guard. Floor 3 ends a group, so the team goes down to the docks anyway. The tests (`PewLootTest`, `DemoTest`, `Demo`, `PewLoot`, `GunNet`, the built-game autopilot) follow; `GunNet` arms floor 3's guards with `ArmedGuards.ForceForTests` to keep its armed-guard checks on the floor with the pew. **The DEMO COMPLETE banner and the README still say the vendor gives the pew "after the demo": they were not rewritten (the owner writes the words); the README (qa_new) was.**
9. **The outpost has a computer instead of a note** (`Outpost/ComputerRules.cs`, `ComputerProp.cs`, `ComputerScreen.cs`; `FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs` `MakeComputer`; the planner's slot kind "computer" on the last monitoring desk; `NoteProp.cs` is gone, the apply moves it into the backup): E on it opens a log-in (user name + password boxes, Enter or the button); the right words (capitals or small letters) come from `ComputerRules.CredentialsFor(runSeed, floor)` (for example GUARD07 / STAPLER42) and are lettered in dots on a **post-it stuck to the monitor's frame** (`PostIt` in the world) and drawn on the monitor's frame in the screen too, so they are in view while typing; after the log-in a page with a **Safes** tab: clicking it lists floor, room and code (or "not on record (needs a crowbar)") of every safe the player has learned, and the computer's own record goes into the journal (J) the first time the tab is opened. Every outpost has a computer; it has a record when the old rules gave the outpost a note (`SafeRules.NoteFor`). Logged-in state lasts for the run (`SafeIntel.LogIn`). Purely local (every player logs in for himself, like reading a note). The wording on the screen is a placeholder. While any outpost screen is up the keys H (hide), T and M (voice) and C (crouch) are gated (`HideSpot`, `VoiceChat`, `WobblyPlayer`) so typing a name does not make the player hide, talk or crouch. The journal's empty text, the How to Play lines and the README were changed only where they said "note".
10. **The key jingle** (`ProceduralSound.Keys`): tuned pitches (E7, G7, A7, B7, D8 with a little detune), softer top (low-pass 8.5 kHz), a soft attack; checked by spectrum (nothing above 10 kHz), never heard.
11. **The end screen's exit button** (`HeistHud.EndButtonRect`): moved up under the banner (at least 150 px from the top, 14 % of the screen) and shown only after 1.2 s (`EndButtonDelay`) so a player hammering attack does not hit it.
12. **Quota pop-ups for everybody** (10): checked by reading, **not changed**: the host announces every banked item (`ElevatorExtraction.Announce` -> `RunSync.BroadcastToast` -> every machine's `ShowToast`, "+$250 banked: Gold Watch") and the "+$X" float on the quota card follows the replicated `Banked`, so every player should see both for anybody's contribution; **not verified with two processes in this round**. Ideas if the owner still sees a gap: the toast is one slot (several items banked together overwrite each other for 3 s) and it does not name who put the item in.

**What was checked** (`BE_qa`, 2026-10-17 15:35-16:25): compile 0 errors; pure tests (the carpet palette, the night sky and windows, the computer's rules, the pistol floors, the key jingle, the hall leaf's openings, the end button, the elevator delay, the toon default); `Looks` (new: `qa_regress.ps1 -Methods Looks:gfx`: numbers and 7-9 pictures per floor of floors 1 and 4 in %TEMP%\be_shot_look*.png: tile / carpet areas, one carpet colour, accent walls, ~120 windows, the city and that it has no collider, glass fronts keep their wall's collider, the outpost computer); `FloatQa` (new: every thing on a surface of floors 1-4 - 2200 to 2600 per floor - is dropped a ray onto the real meshes: **none floats, none stands over nothing, none hangs half over an edge**: the "floating on tables" request found nothing to fix); `Outpost` (82 PASS, the computer's whole flow replaces the note's), `PewLoot`, `Demo`. See "What is done / what is left" for the rest.

**2. Tooling (scratchpad `C:\Users\phick\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-phick-OneDrive-Desktop-BuildingEscape\e7511809-da6a-425a-b8c3-65a911ea9dca\scratchpad`; a COPY of every script is in `DevTools\QaScripts\round3\` because Windows deletes old files under %TEMP%)**
- `BE_dev` = my scratch project copy (the real project + my work). **If it is gone:** `robocopy <real> <scratch>\BE_dev /MIR /MT:16 /XJ /XD Library Temp Logs Builds .git UserSettings obj`, then `touch.ps1 -Roots <BE_dev>\Assets,...` (Windows' temp cleanup deletes OLD files), then **a CLEAN Library**: leave `Library` out of the copy (or delete everything in it but `PackageCache`), then run `build.ps1 -Tag x -NoClients` (batch compile + the pure tests: this imports everything). **A Library inherited from another copy made every ScriptableObject catalog "missing script" in a non-batch Editor** (the hub never built, the catalog builders then overwrote the catalogs: fixed in the builders, applied, but start clean anyway). Disk: C: had only ~6 GB free; each copy with Library is about 2 GB; the client copies `BE_dev_c2/c3/c4/late` in my scratchpad can be re-created from `BE_dev`.
- `build.ps1 [-Method <executeMethod>] -Tag t -NoClients` = compile + `NetDevTest.PolicyTest` (357 -> 369 PASS now; a FAIL line is a failed check). `run1.ps1 -Method <NetDevHarness method> -Tag t [-Graphics]` = one Play Mode scenario in batch (graphics needed for `ShotCam` pictures: `be_shot_*.png`). `ui_dev.ps1 -Method <name> -Tag t` = a scenario in a real NON-batch Editor on a hidden desktop (needed for IMGUI pictures: `be_ui_*.png`; the cutscene and the loot scanner need it too). `qa_regress.ps1 -Prefix p -Project BE_dev -NoBuild -Methods @('Offline','Doors','Looks:gfx',...)` runs many scenarios (summary `qa_summary_<p>.txt`; pass the methods as an ARRAY in-process, not through Start-Process). Two-process / five-player runs: `qa_mp.ps1` (DevTools\QaScripts) needs the client copies and a free loopback port (`%TEMP%\be_mp_busy.txt` is the lock).
- `r3_harness.ps1 -Names InvUi,DoorsSlide,CityLook` registers scenarios of `NetDevR3.cs` (master text `NetDevR3.cs.txt`; it copies it into the tree) in `NetDevHarness.cs` (entry point + the offline list + the switch). New scenario = write `<Name>Scenario()` in the master text, add the name to the call.
- `r3_apply.ps1 [-WhatIf]` applies BE_dev to the real project: every file that differs from the real one, with a backup in `DevTools\QaBackups\before_round3_2026-10-17\`; it REFUSES to overwrite a file somebody else changed since the baseline manifest `baseline_real_1745.tsv` (it keeps the manifest current). `manifest.ps1` writes a SHA-256 manifest. `notes_set.ps1 -Row R0 -Status '...'` / `-Append '<text>'` edits section 2.23 of this file by exact match (never overwrite DEVELOPER_NOTES.md whole). `patchlib.ps1` (`Open-Src` / `Rep` exact-match / `Save-Src`, keeps EOL).
- **PowerShell pitfalls hit this session:** a here-string `@' ... '@` cannot contain another `'@` at the start of a line (it ends the outer one: write such scripts with the Write tool, not inline); variables are case-insensitive (`$S` = `$s`); never `sleep`; a scenario's `Burst` / `ILSupport` / `DirectoryNotFoundException` lines in a Unity log are the hollow package cache and harmless (grep for `\[R3\]`, `] saved`, `] FAIL` instead of everything).
- Unity batch runs only ONE instance per project; a long regression in BE_dev blocks compiling in BE_dev.

Then LOOK at the pictures ("see how it works"): leaves have NO colliders (visual only; the planner keeps 1.75 m in front of every doorway clear of furniture, so a 1 m leaf has room; a 2.4 m wide-door leaf might clip, wide doors never failed to slide in the audit), check guards still pass (`Doors` scenario, `Sneak`), check the outpost door with its wired-glass panes and the keypad / alarm panel beside it (`Outpost` 82 PASS). **Done 18:58:** all of that was run on `BE_dev` (see the table), then applied. **4. NOT STARTED: the designs (what I worked out by reading the code; the owner's words are in R1-R9 above)**

*4.1 Rooms, safes, chip (R1, R2)* - an Executive Suite already exists in the code: `OfficeThemes.ThOffice` -> `ExecOffice` (an office at least 5 x 5 m: desk_3, sofa, bookshelves, a `BigSlot("safe")` 50 % of the time). Pick suites AFTER all cells are furnished, in `OfficePlanner.Run()` before `PlaceSafe()`: numbered rooms (`RoomNumbers.IsNumbered`) of theme "office" with w >= 5 and d >= 5, the ones at the building's edge first (`FloorGenerator.OutsideSides`; the planner has `inp.cols/rows/cell`), 2 per floor when the floor has >= 24 cells else 1; add `RoomInfo.role` ("suite" / "server") in OfficeLayout.cs. The Server Room: the largest numbered room whose theme is "server" (`ThServer`: racks along N/E/W, a reserved 1.5 m square for the "server" treasure); if a floor has none, a "storage"/"copy" room; others stay ordinary. Remove the patrol waypoints inside both kinds of room (`layout.waypoints`, `RoomInfo.wx/wz`): a guard must never patrol into a locked room. **Safes:** the owner wants the safes IN the suites, so every office floor 1-12 gets one safe per suite: `SafeRules.HasSafe` -> true for floors 1..12 (`FloorShape.PlanFor` line ~226 feeds `hasSafe`), `layout.safe` becomes a list `layout.safes` (keep `layout.safe` = the first), `SafeRules.Code(runSeed, floor, index)` (index 0 = today's), `Cash(floor)` shared out between the floor's safes, `FloorSafe`: static list `All` + `Index` (keep `Active` = the first: the Outpost scenario and TestMode use it), `OutpostController.Bits` (bit 2 + index for "safe open"), `RunSync.Outpost.cs` safe RPCs carry the index (bump `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` to "14"), `SafeIntel` entries keyed by (floor, room), the computer's Safes tab lists the current floor's safes (room, code or "not on record (needs a crowbar)": `NoteGivesCode` per safe), `TestMode` lines (6) and `NetDevOutpost.cs` (17 lines: it loops `while (!SafeRules.HasSafe(seed,1)) seed++`, expects the cash of one safe = $2150 = SafeRules.Cash(1), and "a floor without a safe": use the docks / floor 13 now). **Glass:** force a glass front AND glass doors on suites (`FloorGenerator.Glass.cs BuildOfficeWindows` rolls a chance per room: take the roll away for suites). **Painted numbers:** for a suite's and the server room's doorway skip the dark plate (`RoomNumbers.Plates` / `FloorGenerator.RoomNumbers.cs AddPlate`) and letter the number straight on the wall over the door in gold dots ("almost as if painted on"); small gold "EXECUTIVE SUITE" / "SERVER ROOM" line above it. **Chip:** a new small valuable built like `BuildValuable` (FloorGenerator.Valuables.cs: parts + BoxCollider + Rigidbody + Pickup(label "Server Chip", weight 0.12, bankValue) + Throwable) as `BuildChip(...)`, kept OUT of `Valuables.All` (so tests that count the catalog's 13 kinds and the random picker never see it), worth about 0.2 x the floor's quota; its place: a "chip" `Slot` in the server room on a small table (like `MakeComputer`'s `lift` table when the slot is on the floor: `FindFreeReach` spot) with a glowing case; the scanner (hold Z) lists it as any priced Pickup.
*4.2 Door index map (needed by locks, computer, Nav)* - `DoorManager` order = `layout.walls` order over boxes of kind "header" AND "lockheader" (`FloorGenerator.BuildDoors`). New pure class `FloorDirectory` (Interior/): per numbered room `{label (RoomNumbers.Of), RoomInfo, bounds, theme, role, door indices}` built from the layout (match each `RoomInfo.doors` rect to its beam with the same overlap rule as `DoorSlide.SwingSign` / `RoomNumbers.BeamOver`), plus `Find(label)` (trim, case-insensitive), the suites, the server room, the outpost, the speakers (4.7). The generator builds it after `BuildDoors` and keeps `FloorDirectory.Current` (cleared with the floor).
*4.3 Computer locks the doors (R4, R6)* - `SlidingDoor.ComputerLocked` (separate from `Locked`, the outpost's hack lock): a locked door is shut at once (`SetOpen(false)`; stays shut while something is in the doorway, then the lock engages), `GuardNear` is forced false (DoorManager.Update), E says "Locked - open it at the security computer" (DoorManager.HandleUse / OnGUI), officers cannot open it. Host authority + replication, modelled on the doors' (`RunSync.Requests.cs` doors, `RunSync.Events.cs DoorSetRpc`): `RequestComputerLock(string roomLabel, bool lock)` -> host resolves through `FloorDirectory`, checks the sender is the seated, logged-in computer user (4.4) and within reach -> `ComputerLockRpc(ushort[] doors, bool locked, byte epoch)` to everybody; a machine that joins late asks for the full locked set (extend `DoorStatesRequestRpc`): suites and the server room START locked (every machine computes that itself, only on a floor that has an outpost with a computer; never on the boss floor). Sound: loud metallic clang when a suite or the server room is locked, a small click for other rooms, a clunk when unlocked (4.6). **Outpost door lock from inside (R6):** a lever / deadbolt prop inside beside the door (an `Interactable`, like `AlarmPanelProp` in FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs `BuildAlarmPanel`) toggling `ComputerLocked` of the outpost door through the same request; the Hacked tab also accepts "OUTPOST" / "SECURITY". **Guards must not get stuck at a locked door:** add a stuck detector in `GuardAI` (path set, velocity < 0.2 m/s for 3 s, remaining distance > 1 m -> `GoNextPatrol()` / back to patrol) EXCEPT while the PA alert (4.7) holds them at the outpost door.
*4.4 The computer (R3, R4, R5, R8)* - rewrite `Outpost/ComputerScreen.cs` (today: login with a post-it, one Safes tab, plain IMGUI; `ComputerProp.Use` opens it; `OutpostUi.Kind.Computer` freezes the body and frees the cursor; `HudAnim.Id.Computer` = 30 is reserved). States: **Upload** (a button "UPLOAD") -> a loading bar and the line **"Uploading File(s): F_U_BOSSMAN"** (about 2.5 s) -> the existing **sign-in** screen (user / password on the post-it, `ComputerRules.CredentialsFor`) -> a desktop with three tabs **Safes** (as today, per safe), **Nav**, **Hacked**. *Nav:* a 2D map of the current floor drawn from `FloorDirectory` (room rectangles tinted by theme, the numbers as labels (the 3x5 dot font `RoomNumbers.Lit` or IMGUI text), suites gold, the server room blue, the outpost red, locked doors marked), LIVE markers: every player (username "Player n" / the chosen name, and an icon of their character: a coloured disc with the model's first letter, or the toon model's face from `CharacterCatalog`) from `NetPlayer.All` / `WobblyPlayerRegistry.All`, every officer from `GuardAI.All` (puppets have positions on clients), the speakers (4.7) as clickable microphone icons (select one / several / all; buttons SELECT ALL and DESELECT ALL). *Hacked:* a terminal look: green on black, prompt "ROOM NUMBER >" then "LOCK or UNLOCK >" (case does not matter, trim, accept "507" and "507a"), answers "ROOM 507 LOCKED" / "NO SUCH ROOM"; one command = the room number and the verb. *Sounds (R5):* a key clack for every typed character in the login boxes and the terminal (an IMGUI `Event.KeyDown` with a character), a mouse click for every button; new `SoundId`s (4.6). **"Sitting down":** `ComputerScreen.Open` also seats the player: ask the host (`RequestSit`, one user at a time, "somebody is already at the computer"), teleport to the chair spot with `WobblyPlayer.TeleportTo` (NEVER write `transform.position`: 7 rules) and hold still (`HoldStill(true)`), release on Esc / walking away / custody; replicate `seated` and `micPressed` as owner-written `NetworkVariable<bool>` on `NetPlayer` like `crouched` / `hidden`. The chair: `ThOutpost` puts a `chair_office` 0.30 m in front of each monitoring desk; the computer is `TopSlot(desks[last], 0.5, 0)`; the monitor faces local -z of the computer root (`MakeComputer`: screen at z = -0.18, keyboard at +0.10), so the user stands at `computerRoot + root.forward * 0.8` facing the screen. *Pose:* the toon people (`CharacterAnimator.UseClips`, no sit clip) get a procedural sit in `LateUpdate` (`PlayerVisual`): lower the model ~0.5 m, rotate the thigh bones 90 degrees forward and the shins back about the character's right axis (world-space `Quaternion.AngleAxis`), and a two-bone IK reaching the right hand to the microphone button when speaking (analytic law-of-cosines solver on `Animator.GetBoneTransform(HumanBodyBones.RightUpperArm/LowerArm/RightHand)`; `CharacterAnimator.RightHand` already exists); Party-character rigs (not humanoid) only sit lower. *Mic prop (R9):* a desk microphone (stand + capsule + a red button) beside the computer built from primitives in `MakeComputer`; its button target is where the hand goes.
*4.5 Where the pieces hook in* - `Backpack`, `DoorManager.CanUse`, `Crosshair.MenuOpen`, `VoiceChat.Update` already gate on `OutpostUi.Open`; `CameraFollow` frees the cursor for `OutpostUi.FreeCursor`. Use `HudTheme` / `MenuKit` for buttons, a monitor-looking frame for the screen (the current one draws its own bezel + post-it; keep the post-it).
*4.6 Sounds* - append to `SoundId` (enum order = `SoundBank.GeneratedVariants` array order: append at the END of both and add cases in `Build`): MetalLock (loud clang: `OutpostSound.Clunk` + a ringing steel partials like `CrowbarSnap`), LockClick, Unlock, KeyClack (variants 0-3: short noise + low thump), MouseClick, PaOn, PaOff. Check by spectrum like `NetDevRound2Test` does (`Goertzel`).
*4.7 Speakers and the microphone (R7, R8, R9)* - **speakers:** ceiling props in the corridors, planned purely (corridor rooms `kind == 2`: one per ~13 m of hallway, at most 48 so a `ulong` mask covers them), deterministic, in `FloorDirectory.Speakers` (position, id); drawn small (a grey box + a grille) and as icons on the Nav map. **Speaking:** while seated and logged in, HOLD M (`VoiceChat.muteKey` is already gated while an outpost screen is up) = the PA is live: `VoiceChat` sends each captured packet a SECOND time as a PA packet (new named message `be.pa`: claimed client id, codec, seq, length, bytes, `ulong` speaker mask) besides the normal voice, so teammates in the room also hear the speaker acoustically; the host relays a PA packet to every client whose body is within ~28 m of ANY selected speaker (reuse `VoiceRules` with a PA curve: full to 6 m, silent from 28 m), and the listener plays it through ONE `VoiceVoice` per PA speaker with gain = the best of `Gain` over the selected speakers and the pan toward the nearest one (`VoiceVoice.SetMix`); the engine's 3D never applies to `OnAudioFilterRead` audio (2.20). Key-hold alone makes the PA live (the mic level is not needed: tests cannot talk); the voice level still feeds the guards through `VoiceNoise` as always. PaOn / PaOff clicks at the selected speakers. Also work offline (PLAY SOLO: no NetworkManager): the officers' reaction and the clicks must run without RunSync. **Officers (R7):** when the PA is live and ANY officer is within hearing range of ANY selected speaker (about 22 m), every officer that is not `GuardState.Pinning` (and not Dead) is called to the outpost: `GuardAI.Dispatch(outpostDoorPosition)` style (new `PaAlert(Vector3)`: abort a call in progress (`CancelCall` without investigating), leave a chase, walk to the outpost's door), stay alerted for ~25 s after the last PA packet, never enter a room whose door is computer-locked (they stand at the door: the door's blocker collider is shut and `GuardNear` is false), then go back to patrol; armed floors: the same. Host-only logic (`HeistManager` or a new `PaDirector` on the floor); the host learns the PA state from the seated player's requests (`RequestPa(on, mask)`, rate limited, sender must be the seated computer user).
*4.8 R13 signs (the owner: "There should be more signs displaying where which rooms are", then "Don't add too many directional signs where it's easy but make them more valuable so if you find one you can definitely find the correct room")* - not more of the one-block signs: **fewer, complete wayfinding boards**. Plan: (1) a FLOOR MAP board (a wall sign about 3 x 1.6 m with a YOU ARE HERE marker) at the elevator lobby and at 3 other well-spread corridor cells (use `DirectionSigns.FindSites` / `Plan` placement and `FloorGenerator.Signs.cs`): the board shows the 8 x 5 cell grid, the corridors (`DirectionSigns.IsOpen`), every cell's room-number range (the numbering follows the corridors, so a cell's rooms are consecutive: `RoomNumbers.Of`), the elevator, the security office (red), the executive suites (gold) and the server room (blue); rotate the map so "up" = the way the viewer looks at the wall; draw it into a generated `Texture2D` with the 3x5 pixel font at 2-3x scale (about 960 x 480), one material per board; (2) the existing "ROOMS 111-120 ->" signs stay as they are (no more of them); (3) add "EXECUTIVE SUITES" and "SERVER ROOM" as two more targets of `DirectionSigns.PlanSecurity`-style signs (3 each at most, like the SECURITY OFFICE ones, `DirectionSigns.SecurityAreas`). Tests: pure checks on the plan (a board in the lobby cell, 4 boards, each cell's range text consistent with `RoomNumbers`), a picture per board.
*4.9 R11 (less blocky, R.E.P.O.-like world, funky characters)* - nothing done. Findings: no post-processing package was looked for yet (`Packages/manifest.json`; built-in render pipeline; custom shaders live in `Resources/Shaders` e.g. `ElevatorMirror.shader`); the look is flat because lights are mostly `ShadowCastingMode.Off`, walls / floors are plain colours, door leaves 12 cm thick, props from primitives, no trims. Ideas in order of value: a cheap post stack (SSAO-like darkening in corners, soft bloom on the lit ceiling panels and the window lights, colour grading, vignette) as an optional `OnRenderImage` effect or PPv2 if the package is in the cache; real shadows from the few active lights (`FloorLights`, `maxActiveLights`), baseboards / door casings / crown moulding / ceiling grid as ONE merged mesh per floor (`MeshBuilder`), vertex-colour corner darkening on the partitions, slightly smaller door leaves, a normal-ish noise texture on walls and carpet (`FloorGenerator.Surfaces.cs` has the generated textures), warmer practical lights. Make it a setting (Low / High) and measure the frame time (the built game ran 70-107 fps in the elevator).
