**How characters work today (read before A6):** `CharacterCatalog` (`Resources\CharacterCatalog.asset`, built by `Editor\CharacterCatalogBuilder.cs` = menu "Employees Take Revenge > Setup Characters") holds `ToonCharacter { id, label, prefab, role (Player / Guard / Other), clips (Male / Female / Zombie clip set) }` plus the pack's colour `atlas` and the three `ToonClipSet`s (Humanoid clips: any set plays on any Humanoid character). `ToonCharacterFactory.Attach(host, look)` dresses a capsule: instantiates the prefab, puts ONE Standard material with the atlas on every renderer (the pack's own material is URP), fits the model to 1.85 m (`FitScale`), adds `CharacterAnimator.UseClips`. `PlayerAppearance.DefaultToon(slot)` / `ForSlot` choose the default per slot, `IntroCinematic.ToonCycle / ToonOf / PickToon / BossModelId` cast the cutscene, `CharacterCatalog.Players()` feeds the Customize list and the lobby stage. **So A6 = new `ToonCharacter` entries for the 18 Synty people (role Player, clips by gender, a per-character `atlas` override = the Synty `_Albedo_Map`), the Tiny Toon people lose the Player role (they stay for the guards and the zombie), the defaults and the cutscene cast point at Synty ids, and the labels become "Dock worker N" (A8).**

**A6 / A7 findings: the Synty office pack (looked at 2026-10-17 19:30 in the QA tree; nothing built from it yet).**
- Where: imported into `BE_qa` at 19:24 in batch (`Unity.exe -batchmode -nographics -projectPath <tree> -importPackage <the .unitypackage> -quit`, 112 s; a copy of the package is `synty_office.unitypackage` in the scratchpad) as `Assets\Synty\PolygonOffice` and `Assets\Synty\PolygonGeneric` (the shared dependency) and `SyntyPackageHelper`: 3680 files, 209 MB. **It is NOT in the real project yet** (the owner imports it in their Editor, or copy `Assets\Synty` with its .meta files at the apply: new files only, nothing existing is touched).
- **The materials are Shader Graph materials** (`PolygonShader.shadergraph`, `Generic_Basic`, ...). **They work now** that the owner installed Shader Graph: every graph in the pack has a **BuiltIn target** next to the Universal one, all 14 import and report `isSupported` in the QA tree, and the contact sheets (`Editor\SyntySheet.cs`, `run_method.ps1 -Gfx -Method BuildingEscape.EditorTools.SyntySheet.Batch`, env `SYNTY_SHEET_DIR`, `SYNTY_REIMPORT=1` forces a re-import) show 18 people, 34 props and the atlas variant with 0 magenta. **GOTCHA (cost an hour): the Shader Graph package has a file whose path is too long for the QA tree under the long scratchpad path** (`...\Library\PackageCache\com.unity.shadergraph@...\Editor\Generation\Targets\BuiltIn\Editor\ShaderGraph\Templates\SharedCode.template.hlsl` > 260 characters): the importer throws `DirectoryNotFoundException`, every graph becomes `Hidden/GraphErrorShader2` and everything Synty is magenta. Fix: the QA scripts now run the tree through a short junction (`cmd /c mklink /J C:\bq <scratchpad>\BE_qa`; `run_method.ps1`, `qa_regress.ps1`, `ui_bq.ps1`, `qa_chain.ps1`, `qa_final.ps1`, ... use `C:\bq` when it exists). The real project's path is short enough. The runtime-material-swap plan that stood here is NOT needed (do not write `SyntyMaterials.Fix`); the people still get one Standard material with the pack's atlas (`ToonCharacter.atlas`, `ToonCharacterFactory.AtlasMaterialFor`) because it looks the same and needs no shader-graph variants. Old text: They carry two textures, `_Albedo_Map` (the colour atlas) and `_Emission_Map`, plus `_BaseColor` / `_Emission_Color`. Plan: a runtime, non-destructive fix (the rule: never edit pack assets): a small `SyntyMaterials.Fix(GameObject)` that swaps every material whose shader is not supported for a cached `Standard` material (`mainTexture` = `_Albedo_Map`, emission map and colour from the two others, the same for the glass variants with alpha < 1), called where props are made (`FloorGenerator.MakeProto`) and where characters are made (`ToonCharacterFactory`); then the render audit (`qa_chain` audit stage) must still say 0 pink for what the game uses.
- **Characters:** `Assets\Synty\PolygonOffice\Prefabs\Characters`: 18 people, ready to play: `SM_Chr_Boss_Female_01`, `SM_Chr_Boss_Male_01`, `SM_Chr_Business_Female_01..04`, `SM_Chr_Business_Male_01..04`, `SM_Chr_Cleaner_Female_01`, `SM_Chr_Cleaner_Male_01`, `SM_Chr_Developer_Female_01..02`, `SM_Chr_Developer_Male_01..02`, `SM_Chr_Security_Female_01`, `SM_Chr_Security_Male_01`, plus attachments (`SM_Chr_Attachment_Headset_01/02`, `_Identity_01`, `_Nametag_01`, `_Pens_01`, `_Antlers_01`). Each prefab is a full rig (69 transforms, 18 SkinnedMeshRenderers = the modular parts, one Animator) and the model `PolygonOffice\Models\Character.fbx` is **Humanoid** (`animationType: 3`), so the Kevin Iglesias humanoid clips (`KiRetarget`, 2.2x) can drive them like the other human characters. To do: register them in `CharacterCatalog` (ids e.g. `syn_business_f_01`, role Player; the catalog builders are in `Assets\Editor\CharacterCatalogBuilder.cs`, which now leaves an existing asset alone: extend it, do not delete), make `PlayerAppearance.DefaultToon` / `ForSlot` pick from the Synty people (the Tiny Toon people stay in the catalog for the guards: police, zombie), the Customize list shows the Synty people, the lobby stage and the cutscene (`IntroCinematic.ToonCycle`, `ToonOf`, `PickToon`) use them; scale / height of a Synty person against the game's capsule (check `PlayerVisual`), the idle / run / jump clips and the dock-worker pose of the cutscene, the shadows. **Decision still open: the guards (police toon) next to Synty low-poly players: only the players were asked for.**
- **Props:** `Assets\Synty\PolygonOffice\Prefabs`: Buildings 128, Characters 24, Generic 19, Props: Bathroom 15, Desk Props 231, Exterior 19, Furniture 63, Kitchen Props 51, Misc 139, Roof Props 60, Wall Props 60. For A7 map the keys of `Interior\PropTable.cs` (desk_1..3, chairs, monitors, keyboard, mouse, phone, stapler, papers, bookshelves, cabinets, counters, tables, sofas, plants, printer / copier, water cooler, coffee machine, whiteboard, painting, clock, trash bins, ...) to Synty prefabs: new `Add(...)` paths and the measured sizes (the generator logs `[Rooms] '<key>' measures ...` for every mismatch; `FloatQa` finds floating items), `frontQ` per prefab (which side faces +Z), `FloorGenerator.MakeProto` already measures bounds and strips colliders; the `OfficeCatalog` (`Resources\OfficeCatalog.asset`, built by `OfficeCatalogBuilder`) lists the prefabs by key: add the Synty ones there. The Buildings folder has modular wall / floor / ceiling / window pieces: the floors' own structure (partitions, outer walls with windows, carpet, tile) is procedural today; whether to swap it for the kit is a design question for the owner.

**RESEARCH for the layout remake (web, 2026-10-17; vendor guides and fire-code summaries, so the numbers are rules of thumb): ** open plan: main aisles 1.2-1.5 m (never under 0.9 m), secondary aisles 0.9 m minimum and 1.2-1.4 m comfortable, back-to-back desks 1.5 m between the two chair zones, desk fronts facing each other at least 1.2 m apart, plan for the chair (it sticks out 0.45-0.6 m behind the desk edge), 7.5-9 m2 per person; ([OF2go desk space planning](https://www.officefurniture2go.com/content/pdfs/OF2go_Desk_Space_Planning_Top10_QA.pdf), [Open Office Layout Standards](https://arcedior.com/blog/open-office-layout-standards-clearances-2025)). Break room: the appliance counter along a long wall with 1.2 m (48 in) of aisle in front of it, 1.05 m (42 in) from the back of a seated chair to the nearest wall or counter, 0.75 m clear beside the sink, a 1.5 m turning circle, 15-20 % of the headcount at the lunch peak, a central location ([Rayon kitchenette rules](https://rayon.design/knowledge-base/office-kitchenette/rules), [FOF break room layout](https://www.findofficefurniture.com/plan/break-room-lunch-room.html)). Conference room: table about 2.7 x 1.07 m for 8 (0.6-0.75 m of table edge per person), 1.05-1.2 m behind the chairs (0.9 m the absolute minimum), the display on the end wall with its lower edge 1.07-1.2 m up and a shallow credenza (0.45-0.5 m) under it, 0.9 m+ aisle ([OF2go conference room for 8](https://www.officefurniture2go.com/plan/conference-room-8-people.html)). Fire safety: an extinguisher within 23 m (75 ft) WALKING distance of anywhere, mounted 1.07-1.5 m high, along the main routes and near copy / server / break rooms ([Kord fire extinguisher placement](https://kordfire.com/fire-extinguisher-placement-for-office-guide/)); pull stations and exit signs are code items (pull station about 1.2 m up beside every exit door, an EXIT sign over every exit door and at corridor ends). Core: elevators, stairs and RESTROOMS sit together in the building core; the restrooms next to the elevator lobby, men's and women's on opposite sides of the corridor or side by side; stairwells at corridor ends or opposite the elevators.

**A9 plan (break room) and A7 plan (props): the design is in "2.24 LAYOUT REMAKE" below (written when the work starts).**

**What R1a / R2a changed in the code** (`BE_qa` only so far): `OfficeLayout.cs` `RoomInfo.role`; new `Interior/OfficePlanner.Roles.cs` (`PickRoles`: the numbered rooms are ranked edge-first then by a hash of seed and place; suites = big offices (>= 5 x 5 m), else smaller offices, else meeting rooms, else anything with room; the server room = the biggest "server" room, else storage, copy, else the smallest room left; the patrol stops inside them are removed from `layout.waypoints`; `SuitesFor(cells)`), called from `OfficePlanner.Run()` before `PlaceSafe()`; `OfficePlanner.Outpost.cs` `PlaceSafe` tries the suites first and never the server room; `RoomNumbers.Plate.painted` and `FloorGenerator.RoomNumbers.cs` (a third submesh and material, gold, no plate quad for a painted number); `FloorGenerator.Glass.cs` (a suite always rolls its glass front, takes down the mounted pictures on the wall it windows, and is made "grand" = floor to ceiling; `GlassRoomBounds` for tests); tests: `Editor/NetDev/NetDevR4Test.cs` `RolesTest` (hooked into `NetDevTest.PolicyTest` after `Round2Test`), a suite check in `NetDevRound2Look.cs`.

**The owner's feedback that shaped it (verbatim, in order):** "the conference table has 1 chair on each side rather than the heads and 3 chairs at each head rather than the sides of the table" (fixed: long axis follows the pack's W, 4 chairs per long side + one at each head) / "The layout you had previously of the floors with the old builds was perfect, just with updated assets would be great please" (so NO redesign: every theme keeps its OLD arrangement, only the numbers follow the new sizes) / "And add break rooms and the closed doors for rest rooms and the safety equipment and such" / "Better but some of the desks are too close together, make sure there are natural walkways and such" (open: pods of at most 2 desks across, 1.2+ m round them; the long training room and the long open room were too tight: see "left to do" 1) / "Honestly you do you, we'll trust the process and review at the end".

**What was built (all in `BE_qa`; patches `r4_batch7..11.ps1`, generator `r4_proptable_gen.ps1`, tools below):**

New keys: `vending`, `minifridge`, `fridge2`, `espresso`, `toaster`, `kettle`, `shredder`, `desk_l`, `desk_l_big`, `desk_reception`, `desk_stand`, `chair_exec/cafe/blue`, `sofa_wood`, `armchair`, `beanbag_blue`, `table_round2/long/big`, `conf_table2`, `coffee_table2/3`, `shelf_wide/wood`, `cube_wall`, `cube_wall_tall`, `cube_wall_half`, `divider`, `laptop`, `desk_lamp`, `folder_tray`, `photo`, `trophy_cup`, `name_plate`, server racks, `pallet`, `cart`, `janitor_cart`, `coat_rack`, `arcade`, `jukebox`, `pool_table`, `table_football`, posters / certificate / calendar / cork boards / `projector_screen` / `blackboard`, the safety kit (`extinguisher`, `fire_alarm`, `emergency_axe`, `fire_detector`, `sign_exit`, `sign_stairs`, `sign_kitchen`, `sign_wc_men`, `sign_wc_women`, `sign_wc`, `sign_out_of_order`, `sign_manager` ...), `plant_*` variants, `rug_*`. `OfficeCatalogBuilder.BuildBatch` rebuilds `Resources/OfficeCatalog.asset` (batch: `run_method.ps1 -Method BuildingEscape.EditorTools.OfficeCatalogBuilder.BuildBatch`). `OfficeLayout.cs`: `RoomCtx.HideMinHeight = 0.7` (was 0.9: Synty desks and tables are 0.75 m, they must still count as cover), `RoomCtx.CanPlaceRect`.
2. **Planner** (`Interior/OfficePlanner.cs` Run): plans EVERY cell first, then `OfficePlanner.Core.cs` `EnsureRestrooms` + `EnsureBreakRoom`, then furnishes each cell with the random numbers its planning left off at (so a floor is unchanged where the core rooms do not touch it). `CellCtx.pairs` records two stacked 6.4 x 3.1 rooms of a quadrant.
3. **A10 restrooms = locked doors**: per floor two rooms (the stacked pair nearest the elevator, else the two enclosed rooms nearest it) get theme `restroom` (Men's, Women's): not furnished, no patrol stop, not numbered (`RoomNumbers.IsNumbered`), their doorway is in `Layout.deadDoors`; `FloorGenerator.Doors.BuildDoors` makes that door `SlidingDoor.Locked` + `Permanent` (never opens, officers do not open it, `DoorManager` prompt says "Locked", the host refuses opening it); a pictogram sign (`sign_wc_men` / `sign_wc_women`, 1.3 m up) beside the door and `sign_out_of_order` on it (`HangDoorSign`).
4. **A9 break room**: `EnsureBreakRoom` converts the best-fitting enclosed room (lounge, conference, training, storage, copy, office, server; nearest the floor's middle) if the maze rolled none >= 4 m with its own door; `PickRoles` never makes it a suite / the server room. `ThBreak`: kitchen run on the north wall (fridge, counter + microwave, sink, counter + kettle, counter + coffee machine + toaster, minifridge), two vending machines (+ clock) on the west wall, two round tables with four chairs each, notice board, bin, extinguisher by the door, a couch + coffee table when the room is >= 6.6 m wide, the sign KITCHEN beside the door.
5. **A11 safety kit**: `CorridorKit` (extinguisher 0.95 m up on about half the corridor arms and twice per long corridor, a pull station beside it 45 %, an emergency axe case 14 %), `StairKit` (the outside of each stairwell landing: axe under the generator's STAIRS plate on the left of the door; on the right EXIT sign, STAIRS sign, fire alarm, extinguisher), the lobby (extinguisher + alarm + axe), extinguishers in the break / copy / server rooms. NOT done: smoke detectors (a ceiling mount needs a pitch rotation the pieces do not have), exit signs at corridor ends, cameras (decor cameras would look like they watch you).
6. **Themes** (`Interior/OfficeThemes.cs`, rewritten from the old file, same arrangements): `Tuck(chair)` / `Dw` / `Dd` instead of old magic numbers; `Art` / `Hang` (posters, certificates, calendar: variety instead of 2 paintings); `PlantKey`; `Station` (desk items placed by explicit non-overlapping slots), `Island` (the pack's cubicle walls: spine, ends, half walls; does nothing when the cluster does not fit), offices (exec: credenza + desk_3 + executive chair + two visitor chairs + sofa + coffee table + shelves), conference (long axis along x, 4 + 4 + 2 heads, TV + credenza), training (desk_1 rows), storage / copy / server (racks), lounge / nook, open / long open, canteen (vending x3), cafe (reception desk as the counter), lobby (reception desk instead of four counters), service. The outpost (`OfficePlanner.Outpost.cs`) got desks at 2.1 m pitch and a wall screen above every other locker.
7. **Tests** (`NetDevR4Test.cs`, hooked in `NetDevTest.PolicyTest`): `CoreRoomsTest` (96 floors: exactly two restrooms, Men's + Women's, dead doors, signs, nothing inside, by the lobby, a break room >= 4 m with a door), `DockWorkersTest`, `RolesTest`, `ElevatorTimingTest`. 8. **Results so far (BE_qa, 20:58):** 392 pure PASS, `FloatQa` 4 PASS (nothing floats), `LootQa` 301 PASS, `Doors` 76 PASS, `Looks` 29 PASS + 1 FAIL (floor 1: only 1 of 2 executive suites got its glass front: the direction signs on the corridor side moved because the new corridor decor changed where they fit; fix: `DirectionSigns.Blocked` must refuse a spot on the perimeter wall of a suite (`room.role == "suite"`, bounds grown by 0.45 m)); `Outpost` / `Sneak` / `Etr` / `Floors` / `Offline` / `Demo` were still running.

**Designs for the other owner asks (not started in code):** **A3** (outpost guard leaves): `FloorGenerator.Outpost.PlaceOutpostGuard` sets `ai.noticeGate = p => room.Contains(p.x, p.z)`; make it `|| (outpostCtl != null && outpostCtl.Unlocked)` so that after the hack the guard sees and hears everywhere (`GuardAI.noticeGate` is read in `OnNoise` line ~208, `CanSee` ~535 and `GuardVision` for the cone), chases out through the door (an unlocked door opens for him) and walks back to his post (`stationary`); test: `NetDevShaft` gate checks still hold while locked. **A4** (Exit after a wipe): `HeistHud.DrawEndButton` shows one button (online: Back to menu = `NetSession.Leave()`, offline: Restart = reload); make it two side by side: offline [Restart] [Exit -> `SceneManager.LoadScene(NetConst.MenuScene)`]; online client [Exit] = `Leave()`; online host [Exit] opens a modal "Leave with party" / "Leave solo" / Cancel: with party = new `NetSession.ReturnPartyToLobby()` (host: `nm.SceneManager.LoadScene(NetConst.MenuScene, Single)`, players / RunSync are spawned with destroyWithScene so they go away, `State = InLobby`, Steam lobby `Unlock()` (needs adding to LobbyService), everybody `SetReady(false)`; clients switch `State` to `InLobby` in `nm.SceneManager.OnLoadComplete` for the menu scene), solo = `Leave()` (the session ends: there is no host migration). Test with the two-process harness. **A5** (trade): **there is NO trade system in this project** (a search for "trade" in `Assets\Scripts` finds nothing), so A5 is a whole new feature, not a pop-up on an existing one. The owner's words: "When a player is near another player they can trade by clicking F while placing their crosshair over the other player and then there should be a cursor pop up saying Confirm or Cancel so that a player doesn't accidentally send a trade request". Proposal: (1) `Crosshair.Aim` gets an `AimKind.Player` (a `WobblyPlayer` of another peer within ~3 m under the crosshair) with the prompt "[F] Trade with <name>"; (2) F opens a small cursor pop-up "Send a trade request to <name>?" Confirm / Cancel (cursor free like the end screen); (3) Confirm = a host RPC to the other player: "<name> wants to trade" Accept / Decline (10 s); (4) Accept = a two-sided trade window (each side offers backpack pieces / cash / the pistol, both press Confirm, the host swaps them atomically and checks both are still in reach; `Backpack` / `RunSync` already move pieces around, see the carry code); open questions for the owner: WHAT can be traded (loot pieces, weapons, cash?), whether trades are allowed while pinned or in custody (no), and whether it only works in a run or also in the docks.

## 3. Things to check when playing

### Custody checks

- Offline: get caught by a chasing guard -> you fall over and the run ends immediately (one player = whole team).
- Two players: one gets pinned -> countdown 12..1 on their screen, label over their head for the other player. The other hits the officer with a weapon (or throws a mug) -> pinned player gets up, officer is stunned.
- Let the 12 s run out with a teammate still free -> "BUSTED", the run goes on. Free player finishes the floor, rides to the docks (the busted player is lying in the car), opens the shop (E), **Buy back** appears at the bottom; the vendor's bubble shows the buy-back line.
- Everyone down -> "BUSTED on <floor>" screen.
- After the first backup call the banner "BACKUP: another officer in N s" counts down and an officer arrives from the guest elevator every 30 s.
- Host + client: the same, from the client's side (client pinned, client buys back).


### New in the latest sessions

- Floor 1+: no music until an officer calls backup (the "!" ring fills, radio squelch); then a track fades in and plays until the elevator seals or the next floor loads. Each floor has a different track.
- In the elevator: lounge music; a ding when the new floor has loaded.
- Doors are shut until a player presses E at one ("[E] Open door"); it stays open until E is pressed again ("[E] Close door"), never closes on a dropped crate or a person; officers open the doors they are about to walk through (on patrol, investigating, chasing) and those close behind them; an officer walking past a door opens nothing.
- Officers jingle keys while moving (faster when running), crackle static now and then, bubble of symbols every few seconds.
- Plates with room numbers over every room's door.
- The elevator car has wood walls, steel trim, carpet, a wood ceiling with downlights, steel doors and a mirror wall that shows the player (section 2.8 lists the checks).
- Night: every floor has a ceiling with light panels; only some rooms and corridors are lit (the lobby by the elevator, the elevator cars and the docks always are), the rest is dim blue-grey, but never black. **Night checks** (never run in Unity): (1) walk through a floor: lit rooms alternate with dim ones, you can always see where you are going; (2) the lit panels glow, the dark ones are grey; (3) the lit rooms have a warm or cool pool of light on the floor, and it fades in and out smoothly as you walk (no popping, no flicker at the edge); (4) the camera never goes above the ceiling, also when looking up in a corner; (5) officers and the flashlight beam are still visible in dark rooms; (6) the docks are brighter, the shop is readable; (7) no pink or flat grey panels in a build; (8) frame rate is as before (if not, lower `maxActiveLights`); (9) boss floor is fully lit; (10) floor 12's hidden hallway has lights and a ceiling.
- **Door checks** (never run in Unity): (1) walk up to a door: nothing opens, the prompt "[E] Open door" appears when the crosshair is on it within ~2 m; (2) E opens it with the sound, walk through, walk away: it stays open; (3) E at it again: "[E] Close door", it closes; (4) stand right beside the door and press E: it closes at once; stand in the doorway and press E: it must not shut on you (prompt: "Something is in the doorway"); (5) with the crosshair on an item in reach E picks it up, on the door it works the door, on the dealer it talks; (6) nothing happens in menus; (7) an officer walking along a corridor of doors opens none of them; an officer who walks into a room (on patrol, going to look at a noise or at where he lost you, running at you as backup, chasing) has the door open before he reaches it and it shuts behind him; run the editor scenario `NetDevHarness.Doors` and read the `guards:` lines; (7b) chase: stay in a guard's cone (the fan on the floor) for a long time and he keeps after you; get out of the fan for 5 s in a row (a glimpse starts the 5 s again) and he gives up and walks to where he last saw you; (8) host + client: open a door on one machine, the other sees and hears it; a client joining a floor late sees doors the host already opened; (9) the pack's "Nothing there - aim the crosshair at an item" message does not show while the crosshair is on a door or the dealer.
- **Crosshair**: a reticle in the middle of the screen; E picks up / works a door / talks to the dealer only for what it is on (section 2.9 has the checklist); a melee swing goes towards it.
- **Guard outposts** (section 2.11 has the full checklist): the room at the end of the floor with the long dark window and a keypad by its steel door; E on the keypad starts a four-key W/A/S/D hack (1 s per key); a miss sets off the alarm and calls backup; inside are desks, lockers and a table with notes / weapons / valuables; notes name the room of a floor safe (and sometimes its code; J = journal); a safe without a code is pried open with a crowbar (35% it breaks); a safe holds half the floor's quota in cash bundles.
- **Alarm panel, signs, loot** (section 2.12 has the checklist): a red button beside the inside of the outpost's door that shuts the siren off and calls the backup off (10 s radio jam); "ROOMS 111-120" signs with arrows on the corridor walls; about 50% more things to steal, spread so most rooms have something.
- **Floor-4 demo** (section 2.17 has the checklist): guards carry a pew from floor 4; a pew lies in floor 4's outpost; the elevator never goes above floor 4; after floor 4 the docks say DEMO COMPLETE and the vendor gives the pew away.
- Host + client: bubbles, sounds, music are local, so the other player's screen is never required to match, but both must hear the same intense track on the same floor. Doors a player works are shared (host decides): open one on the client and the host sees it open too, and the other way round; officers' automatic opening is local.

## 4. Other work in the repo (done by the brother, commit 6738b81)

- **Loading dock** (`FloorGenerator.Dock.cs`): three garage doors, two shut, the third open with the vendor's trailer backed in; the shop is inside the trailer; punching bag, clutter. Matches the request.
- **Vendor speech bubble** (`VendorChat.cs`): was a placeholder ("Shocked you made it back"); fixed this session to the requested line.
- **Purple (pink) materials**: `Editor/RenderAudit.cs` plus ~100 materials converted. A static scan of every `.mat` found no URP/shader-graph shaders left (109 Standard, 4 on a project shader, 1 skybox) and all runtime `Shader.Find` calls use `Standard` with a legacy fallback. Not seen on screen this session, so run the audit once. Also check *Project Settings > Graphics > Always Included Shaders* contains `Standard` for builds.
- Voice: guards hear you by loudness (`VoiceNoise.LevelFromRms`, 3 m at the mic gate to 30 m at a shout). The commit titled "Added pinned state start" contains only this voice work; there was no pinning code in the repo before this session.

## 5. Audit report (multiplayer, reliability, readability, efficiency)

Everything under `Assets/Scripts` and the editor tools was read. Fixed (batch be32):

- **Floor loading**: `TowerManager.LoadFloor` can no longer leave `Loading` set after an exception (the elevator would be dead for the rest of the run); a load asked for while another is running is queued, so two floors are never generated at once. `TowerManager` / `HeistManager` clear their `Instance` on destroy.
- **Multiplayer**: a disconnect re-checks "everybody down"; teammates' puppets no longer stumble twice; Rigidbody interpolation is only on for the body this machine drives (it fought the network transform on puppets); `NetPlayer.IsLocalPlayer` no longer hides the Netcode member of the same name; client puppets that already arrived are not rewritten every frame (loot kept the physics engine busy); the voice receive path reuses its buffer; `ProtocolVersion` bumped.
- **Unity-null safety**: `?.` / `??` on Unity objects replaced with `TryGetComponent` / `if (obj)` where a destroyed object could slip through.
- **Allocation-free UI**: the old HUD made a new native `GUIStyle` for every label on every GUI event (several times a frame). `HudText.Label` now draws on Repaint only with one scratch style; `HudStyles.Label(size, anchor, fontStyle[, colour])` hands out cached styles. About 40 per-event styles were removed. Non-interactive HUDs draw on Repaint only; `GUI.matrix` is restored by the lobby menu and invite popup.
- **Per-frame work**: pickup search is allocation-free (it also had a 64-collider buffer that could miss items in cluttered rooms; now 256); camera-rig lookups are throttled; guard vision cones rebuild every 2nd frame per guard; the crouch scale is only written while it changes; elevator banking returns early when empty; the panel click ray uses a buffer.
- TestMode's level grid had 3 rows (12 buttons) for 14 levels; it now sizes itself.

Checked and fine, no change: custody and backup rules, vendor buy-back, all `Request*` validation on the host, state mirror gating, snapshot format and epoch, NetId assignment, floor checksum, join/leave handling, scene builders, voice.

**Known limitations, deliberately left (decide before a public release):**

- Events (grab, store, consume, spawn) that reach a client **while it is still building the floor** are dropped. Only matters in the first seconds after a floor change; the floor checksum shows the damage. A client-side queue keyed by floor epoch would fix it.
- A player who disconnects loses the loot in their pack (it is destroyed with them).
- Elevator, vendor and hide-spot triggers use `OnTriggerEnter/Exit` per collider; an object with several colliders crossing the edge can flicker in and out for a moment, and a destroyed collider sends no exit.
- Not applied for lack of a profiler: static batching of the floor's boxes, instancing of repeated props.
- `NetPlayer.prefab` and `Greybox.unity` still carry old `VoiceNoise` fields (`threshold`, `maxRadius`); the script ignores them (it uses `minLevel`, `radiusAtGate` 3 m, `radiusAtShout` 30 m) and Unity drops them next time the asset is saved.
- `HideSpot` runs an `Update` per instance and `GuardAI` casts vision rays every frame; fine for the current guard counts, first things to look at if frame rate drops on big floors.

## 6. Known risks / follow-ups

- Network tests (`NetDevHarness`, `NetDevQa`) call `CallBackup`; the 30 s repeat can spawn extra guards in a long test and upset guard-count comparisons.
- Custody physics runs on the machine that owns the body; teleporting a busted body into the elevator resets its rotation and `HoldDown` tips it over again.
- A remote client is not stopped by the host from sending grab/store requests while in custody (only local input is blocked). Fine for friends, not for a public build.
- `HeistManager.Caught`, `maxCaught`, `NotifyPlayerCaught` are legacy and unused.
- After-hours lighting (section 2.7) has never been seen in Unity: the ambient / moon / lamp numbers are first guesses, and without shadows the lamps light through walls. A cheap improvement if that looks bad: turn the lamp's spot angle down, or give only the lamps in the player's room shadows (`LightShadows.Hard`, one at a time).
- The crosshair (section 2.9) has never been seen in Unity: the aim tolerances (item box +6 cm, door box +10 cm and the beam, 25 cm / 15 cm wall slack) and the reticle's size and colours are first guesses. If E feels too fussy on small items raise `AimMath.ItemPad`; if a door is hard to hit raise `DoorPad` / `DoorTopExtra`. No settings-page toggle for the reticle yet (`PlayerPrefs` `OvertimeEscape.Crosshair`).
- The officers' doors (section 2.4) and the 5 s chase (section 2.10) have never run in Unity. Things most likely to need tuning: `SlidingDoor.LookAheadSeconds` (0.9 s) / `WalkZone` (0.5 m) if doors open too early or too late for a guard, and `agent.path` (reads the agent's corridor; if `corners[0]` is not the agent's position the code copes, it looks for the leg he is on). A guard that sets off from rest right next to a shut door can still clip the leaves for a moment (the door needs 0.25-0.35 s). A remote client sees puppets turn into doors with a straight-line guess only.
- The guard outposts (section 2.11) are the largest never-run change: the generator, the planner, the one-way window (a second use of the mirror camera), the networked lock / safe state, the hack and the safe screens. `OutpostStep` keeps a failing step from stopping a floor loading, so look for `[Outpost] Could not build ...` in the console first. The numbers most likely to need tuning: the hack window (`HackSession.Window` 1 s, `Grace` 50 ms, `IntroSeconds` 0.6 s), `OutpostController.AlarmSeconds` (14 s), the odds in `SafeRules` (1 in 5, 62% of notes give the code, 35% crowbar break, cash share 0.5) and `OutpostLoot` (weapon chance 40% to 75%, valuables 3.5% of the quota per unit).
- The alarm panel, direction signs and the bigger loot plan (section 2.12) have never run in Unity; the signs were designed without the user's reference picture (it never arrived), so expect a restyle. The loot numbers are simulated, not played: if floors feel flooded, `LootPlan.SmallTarget / LooseCount` are the knobs. NetIds per floor rose by ~35, so any test that hard-codes a pickup count needs updating.
- Nice to have: kneel/pin animation, handcuffed look, pin/bust sounds, tuning of 12 s / 30 s / buy-back price, real music and sound clips.

## 7. Rules of the house

- Scripts are CRLF. If you edit with tools that expect LF, convert, then convert back before saving into the project. New scripts need their `.meta` committed.
- **Never move a player by writing `transform.position`.** The owner's body is a Rigidbody on interpolation, which puts a transform-only move back where the rigidbody was (that is how players ended up outside the map). Use `WobblyPlayer.TeleportTo` (or `NetPlayer.PlaceAt` for a networked player). The editor test scenarios do the same.
- Floor generation must stay deterministic: only `System.Random` for floor content, never `UnityEngine.Random`; creation order matters for `Net.NetIds`. Clients regenerate identical floors from the host's seed. Cosmetic extras (doors, plates, bubbles, sounds) take no NetIds and must not. Content that depends on the run but not on the floor's random numbers (the outposts' loot, the safes, the notes) comes from `SafeRules.Hash(intelSeed, ...)`, never from the floor's `rng`, so it cannot disturb the maze, loot or guards.
- **Edit mode**: `FloorPreviewWindow` calls `Generate` outside Play mode. Code in the generator that needs the running game (components with `Awake`, `Destroy`, audio, the controller, the window, `FillOutpost`) is guarded with `Application.isPlaying`; use the generators' `Kill(...)` instead of `Destroy` for objects made while building.
- The host runs all rules; clients send `Request*` calls (`RunSync.Requests.cs`) and receive events/state (`RunSync.Events.cs`, `RunSync.State.cs`). `Net.NetRole.Simulates` = offline or host.
- **IMGUI**: use `HudStyles` / `HudText.Label` for any new on-screen text, never `new GUIStyle` inside `OnGUI`. Draw non-interactive things on `EventType.Repaint` only. Restore `GUI.matrix` / `GUI.color` if you change them. With GUILayout, draw the same controls on Layout and Repaint.
- **Unity null**: do not use `?.`, `??` or `is { }` on `UnityEngine.Object`s; use `TryGetComponent` or `if (obj)`. Clear a singleton's `Instance` in `OnDestroy`. `OnTriggerExit` is not sent for destroyed objects. Rigidbody interpolation stays off on kinematic puppets moved by the network.
- Pure-logic code (`Scripts/Interior/*` including `FloorShape`, `DotText`, `DirectionSigns`, `LootPlan`, `Scripts/Interaction/AimMath.cs`, `Scripts/Outpost/SafeRules|HackRules|SafeNotes|OutpostLoot`, `CustodyRules`, `MusicPlan`, `ProceduralMusic`, `ProceduralSound`, `DoorLogic`, `SymbolSpeech`) has no Unity types so it compiles and tests outside Unity with plain `csc`; that is the easiest place to add tests.
- Tooling used by the agent sessions (not in the repo): a stub-based compiler check of the runtime and editor code, a .NET console test for the audio/music/door/bubble/room-number logic, and a script that delivers CRLF copies of changed files. Re-create what you need; none of it replaces opening the project in Unity.

### 8.1 Standing rules from the owner (do not break them)

- BuildingEscape is its own game and repo: nothing from other projects. The owner writes the game text (placeholder lines are fine). **Never modify art assets** (models, textures, materials, shaders) unless asked. Do not disturb the owner's open Unity editor: develop and test in a scratch copy, and apply verified files to the real project after checking `git log` / `git status`.
- **Decided 2026-10-16:** a gunshot on a *player* stays the knock-down stumble that is built (no damage, harmless under test-mode god mode).
- **Also from the owner on 2026-10-16 evening:** do not run any git commands (the second agent compared files by SHA-256 and timestamps instead, and the owner commits); "apply when verified": verified work is copied into the real project and the owner is told (their Editor is open on the project and recompiles; keep out of Play Mode while it does). The owner asked to be asked the blocking questions up front.

### 8.2 Task 2: the in-run HUD (plan and what exists)

**Today** the HUD is IMGUI text: `Scripts/HeistHud.cs` (two lines top left: floor, quota, cash, lost, free/pinned/busted, run total; two long controls lines at the bottom; the centre messages for quota met, the docks, the end screens and the Restart / Back to menu button), `Backpack.DrawHud` (weight bar bottom left with a Pack line, the pick-up prompt), `BackupHud` (the "!" ring and the red backup message), `CustodyHud` (BACKUP countdown, big PINNED / BUSTED text, tags over downed teammates), `Net/Voice/VoiceChat.OnGUI` (mic state top right), `Interaction/Crosshair` (reticle and the Interactable prompt), the prompts in `Doors/DoorManager`, `Vendor`, `ElevatorPanel`, `ElevatorExtraction`, and the pew's ammo panel (`Guns/GunController.OnGUI`, a placeholder). All of it uses `HudText.Label` + `HudStyles.Label(size, anchor, fontStyle[, colour])` (cached styles; draw on `EventType.Repaint` only; `HudText.Label` shrinks text to fit and keeps 8 px clear on each side of its rectangle).

**Plan** (what the owner asked for: straight-forward, easy to read, colours that make sense, cash green):

- One new `Scripts/HudTheme.cs`: the palette with fixed meanings (**cash = green** `#5BE37D`, quota / loot value = gold `#FFC94A`, lost = salmon `#FF6B5E`, police / alarm / pinned = red `#FF4D42`, warning (heavy pack, few rounds, backup coming) = orange `#FF9E3D`, information and totals = blue `#6FB6FF`, text `#F2F4F8`, labels `#A9B2C3`), panels and bars (rounded rectangles through `GUI.DrawTexture(..., color, borderWidth, borderRadius)` on `Texture2D.whiteTexture`), text with a soft shadow, key caps, a `Prompt(text, y, colour)` that draws a leading "[E]" as a key cap, and one scale (`Screen.height / 1080`, clamped 0.75-2).
- `HeistHud`: a status card top left (floor name and `n / 14`; **CASH in big green**; **QUOTA as a gold bar** `$banked / $quota`, turning green and "QUOTA MET" when met; LOST in red; RUN TOTAL in blue; on the docks "NEXT QUOTA"; on the boss floor "KNOCK OUT THE BOSS" in red), a chip per player under it (green free, red "PINNED n", grey busted; "YOU" / "P2"...), the controls as a row of key caps along the bottom (shrinks to fit), and one banner for the moments that matter (quota met in green, the docks in blue, "ESCAPED THE TOWER" green, "BUSTED" red with a "backup had been called" line) with the end-screen button below it.
- `Backpack.DrawHud`: a pack card bottom left (weight bar green / orange / red at 65 % / 90 %, the loot in the pack in gold, "IN HAND <item>"); every prompt (`Backpack`, `DoorManager`, `Vendor`, `Crosshair`, `ElevatorPanel`, `ElevatorExtraction`) goes through `HudTheme.Prompt`. `GunController` ammo panel bottom right in the same colours (white count, orange at 3 rounds or fewer, "RELOADING" with a bar). `BackupHud` / `CustodyHud` / `VoiceChat` messages become panels or chips in red / orange. The vendor's shop window header (cash green, prices gold) is a nice extra.
- **Keep** every number and rule the HUD reads (`HeistManager.Cash/Banked/Quota/Lost/RunTotal/Surplus/SurplusPaid/Floor/FloorLabel/TotalFloors/Phase/BackupActive/BackupIn/BackupCalled`, `WobblyPlayerRegistry.All`, `WobblyPlayer.Custody/PinLeft`, `NetPlayer.Slot`). **Update, 2026-10-16 evening: the owner wants it to LOOK nicer with better styling, not only meaningful colours, and chose the cartoon / toon look: chunky dark outlines, bright saturated fills, bouncy pop-in animation.** The plan above says what goes where and what each colour means; the toon kit that gives it the look is section 2.14.

**Status (2026-10-16 evening): built, tested in a gallery and in the real game, and applied to the repo** (files in 8.5). What is left of task 2: the windows that are not HUD (inventory, shop, elevator panel, hack / safe / intel screens, speech bubbles) still have the plain look, and the garbage per frame could go lower (section 2.14, "Cost").
- **Test**: a Play Mode scenario that photographs the real screen (`ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotAsTexture()` after `WaitForEndOfFrame`; a camera render does not include IMGUI) in the docks, on a floor, with the pew out, with an item under the crosshair, quota met, backup called, pinned. The batch run needs graphics (no `-nographics`). Drafts of all of it are in the agent's scratch folder (`scratchpad/ui_draft`: `HudTheme.cs`, `HeistHud.cs`, `NetDevUi.cs`); if they are gone, write them from this section.

### 8.3 Task 3: room numbers that can be learned (plan)

**Today** (section 2.6): every numbered room has a plate over its door; numbers grow in the planner's order (`layout.rooms`, west to east, south to north from the elevator corner), floor 1 = 101..., floor 2 = 201..., and after 99 rooms the numbers go round with a letter (101A...). About 80-130 numbered rooms per floor. The direction signs (2.12) group the numbers in blocks of ten **by number order** and walk the maze to find the nearest room of a block; the safes' notes name a room by its plate (`FloorGenerator.SafeRoomLabel`).

**The owner's goal**: the numbering should make sense to a player who follows it and learns it over time.

**Plan**: (1) **audit first**: dump, for several floors, every numbered room as `number -> cell -> position -> neighbours`, and look at it as a player would (are neighbouring rooms close in number? does a number rise as you walk away from the elevator? do 101A..199A repeat confusingly?). (2) Pick one simple rule a player can pick up, the same on every floor. Candidates: hundreds-of-the-floor prefix kept, then the cell's wing as a letter or tens digit (a block of numbers per 18 m cell), numbers rising along the corridor away from the elevator, odd numbers on one side and even numbers on the other, no letter wrap (use a second digit group per wing). (3) Keep it deterministic (floor content only from the floor's seeded `System.Random`; the plates take no NetIds). (4) Update everything that depends on the numbering: `Interior/RoomNumbers.cs` (`Label`, `IsNumbered`, the plate placement), `FloorGenerator.RoomNumbers.cs`, `Interior/DirectionSigns.cs` (blocks of ten, "ROOMS 111-120"; its probe checks every sign on 400 floors), `FloorGenerator.SafeRoomLabel` and the safe notes, `NetDevTest.RoomNumberTest`, the test-mode "Direction signs" section and the notes in section 2.6 / 2.12. (5) Run the direction-sign and room-number tests; play a floor and walk it by the numbers.

**Status (2026-10-16 evening): done and applied.** The owner said "the plate numbers are good it's the layout, sometimes the room numbers aren't in a good order", so only the order changed (section 2.6): `RoomNumbers.Of(layout, floor)` walks the maze from the elevator (see 2.6 for the rules), `Layout.input` carries the maze, `DirectionSigns.MakeBlocks` uses the same order (blocks of ten are now stretches of corridor; the same number of signs, 45 on the six audited floors, before and after). Checked by: 6 new pure checks in `NetDevTest.RoomNumberTest` (every room once, unique labels, deterministic, a cell's rooms consecutive, numbers only go up walking away from the elevator over 928 cell pairs, 101 near the elevator; 166 PASS in all) and by **`NetDevRoomAudit.RoomAudit`** (`-executeMethod BuildingEscape.EditorTools.NetDevRoomAudit.RoomAudit`, batch, no graphics): it builds six real 8 x 5 floors, prints each floor as a map (north at the top, the cells with their numbers, the elevator at the bottom left), how far apart neighbouring numbers are, the walking check, and the signs, to `%TEMP%\be_room_audit.txt`. Look at the map when judging a rule. Old order on those floors: a number went down in 14% of the pairs; neighbouring numbers 14.2 m apart, 52 of 681 pairs over 30 m; new: 0%; 15.9 m and 76 pairs (a wing's last room and the next wing's first are far apart by nature; the walking rule is what matters). If the owner still finds a spot odd, the knobs are in `RoomNumbers`: the fork order (`TurnKey`), the order inside a cell (`OrderInCell`), where a letter round may start (`Visit`, `Emitter.RoundBreakIfNeeded`).

### 8.4 Task 4: the guards' own elevator, and one guard in the outpost (plan)

**Today**: the guard outpost (section 2.11) is the farthest room of the floor; in its back-west corner is a small **elevator alcove** (`FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs: BuildOutpostCar`, planned by `Interior/OfficePlanner.Outpost.cs`) that the **backup officers arrive in**: `HeistManager.CallBackup(GuardAI)` / `CallBackupAt(where, headline)` -> `FloorGenerator.SpawnReinforcements(target, n)` puts the guards inside the car, its door opens into the room and they walk out through the room and the (locked) front door; clients replay this with `RunSync.BroadcastSpawnGuard` / `SpawnGuardRpc` -> `FloorGenerator.SpawnReinforcementFromNet(id, pos, look)`. A floor without an outpost (the boss floor, or a failed furnishing step) uses the older plain `FloorGenerator.BuildGuestElevator(cell)` and, without any car, `SpawnReinforcementsAnywhere`. The problem the owner reports: a player inside the outpost when backup is called is standing where the guards appear.

**Requirements** (the owner's words are in the table above):

1. A **second elevator shaft only for guards**, from which all backup comes. It must be **aligned on every floor like a real shaft** (the same x / z on every floor, as the players' service elevator is in the corner cell), with its doors opening onto a corridor or lobby of the floor, not into the outpost. The players' elevator is the 8 x 8 m room in the corner cell (`FloorGenerator.BuildElevatorRoom`, `TowerManager`); the guard shaft needs its own cell or room that the planner keeps free on every floor (look at how `FloorShape.PlanFor` / `OfficePlanner` reserve the elevator cell and the "guest cell"), a car with doors (`ElevatorDoor` with `chimeOnOpen`), a spawn point inside, and the NavMesh bake (it is baked after the walls and before the ceiling: see 2.7). Every machine builds the same floor from the host's seed, so the geometry costs no network traffic; creation order of NetIds must stay deterministic (section 7).
2. The outpost always has **exactly one guard inside** (host / offline; clients get a puppet through the normal guard snapshots). He is **stationary on duty** (a post, not a patrol). Once a player is inside, that player has to **knock him out first** (melee, the pew in test mode, thrown items: `GuardAI.WeaponHit`) **before he alerts or pins** them: he must not call backup (`StartCall` / `HeistManager.CallBackup`) or chase and pin (`HeistManager.Pin`) while he is up and has seen a player. Decide with the owner what "alerts" means in detail (a 2 s look like every guard? an instant alarm?). The existing rules for a guard who spots a player are in 2.10 and 2.1.
3. He must **not see players outside the outpost**. His vision (`GuardAI.CanSee`, `GuardVision`) must be blocked until **the outpost door is open and the player is inside the room** (the door is `Doors/SlidingDoor` with `Locked`; the room's rectangle is in `OutpostInfo`; the one-way window is a mirror for the corridor side only). Hearing (noises, voice, gunshots) is a separate decision: ask.
4. When the old alcove goes away, the outpost's furniture plan (`OfficePlanner.Outpost.cs`) gets the corner back; `FloorGenerator.AfterHours.cs` and `OfficeLayout` reference the alcove (ceiling panels, blocks); `NetDevOutpost.cs` and `NetDevQa.cs` have lines that spawn backup through the old car (check them), and `NetDevTest` has planner tests.

**Status (2026-10-16 evening): the base is done and applied (section 2.15). Done too (section 2.16): the owner's late request, sent after the base was built: "There should also be a two doors one on each opposite side of the floor where backup guards can come from. This will prevent players from camping the guard elevator. These doors would represent stairwells."** As built (applied 2026-10-16 evening; defaults chosen without asking, change them if the owner objects): two stairwell landings in the two corners that are neither elevator's (north-west = cell (0, rows - 1) and south-east = cell (columns - 1, 0), the same cells on every floor, not on the boss floor); each a small walled landing (4.8 m outside) with a steel double door onto its cell's corridor, a "STAIRS" sign, three steps and an exit lamp inside; the planner treats them like the guards' car (a solid block and a lane) and the outpost goes to the farthest cell that is none of the three; a backup call uses the guards' elevator **and** the stairwells: the doors are ordered by distance from the free players (those more than 12 m from every free player first, in random order, the one used last time last), and the guards of one call spread over different doors when there are several; the doors' open / shut state replicates as one byte (`RunSync.State`, replacing the elevator's bool, protocol "9"). Tests (all passing): 7 pure planner checks (corners, distinct cells, the outpost in none of them, doors face open neighbours, landings and lanes clear) and `Shaft` / `ShaftNet` scenario checks (the stairwells exist on every floor, backup comes out of the doors farthest from the player, camping at the elevator or at a stairwell makes the guards come from the other doors, the doors take their turns, the door byte reaches the client).

**Answered by the owner (2026-10-16): the shaft goes in the OPPOSITE CORNER from the players' elevator.** **Defaults chosen for the rest (change them if the owner objects):** the guards just step out of closed doors with a chime (no visible moving car; every floor is built on its own, so "aligned" means the same x / z on every floor); one stationary guard per outpost, unarmed unless the pew's floor rule arms him; he neither sees nor hears anything outside the outpost until its door is open and a player is inside (the siren excepted).

### 8.6 How the work is verified (the method; reference copies of the scripts are in `DevTools\QaScripts`)

- Unity batch mode on a **scratch copy** of the project, never the owner's open project (Unity locks a project): `Unity.exe -batchmode [-nographics] -projectPath <copy> -executeMethod BuildingEscape.EditorTools.<Class>.<Method> -logFile <log>`. Pure tests: `NetDevTest.PolicyTest` (all `[NetTest] policy ...: PASS`). Play Mode scenarios: `NetDevHarness.Offline / Doors / Outpost / Sneak / Floors / Toon / Pew / PewLook` (single process; `-nographics` is fine except for pictures: `PewLook`, `Toon`, `Shots` and the HUD pictures need graphics) and the multi-process ones, one Unity process per player with one project copy each: `Qa`, `Spawn`, `Armed`, `OutpostNet` (`BE_QA_ROLE` = `host` / `c1`..`c4` / `late6`, `BE_PLAYERS`, `BE_RUN`). Look for `DONE` and the absence of `FAIL`. **A multi-process run shares the signal files `%TEMP%\be_sig_*.txt` and the loopback port with every other session on the machine: never start one while another session's multi-process run is going** (`ClearSignals` deletes every `be_sig_*.txt`, and a client can join the wrong host). **Agreed protocol (night of 2026-10-16, the Q/A session and the triage session):** write `%TEMP%\be_mp_busy.txt` (who and what) before a multi-process run and delete it afterwards; wait while it exists (ignore it when it is older than 20 minutes) and until no Unity batch run with `-executeMethod` is going; every session keeps its own client copy (never overwrite a client copy another session may be running in). Without a graphics device the scenarios skip their pictures (`SKIP picture ...`) instead of crashing Unity.
- **Pictures of IMGUI (the HUD)**: not possible in batch mode (`OnGUI` is never called there, in an Editor or in a player). Run a real, non-batch Editor on a spare project copy **on a hidden Windows desktop** so it does not show on, or take focus from, the owner's screen: a small PowerShell wrapper calls `CreateDesktopW` and `CreateProcessW` with `STARTUPINFO.lpDesktop = "WinSta0\<name>"`, waits with a timeout and kills the process if needed (**saved in the repo as `DevTools/RunOnHiddenDesktop.ps1`, with its usage; it is outside `Assets`, so Unity ignores it - the owner decides whether to commit it**). The scenario maximizes the Game view (`EditorWindow.GetWindow(typeof GameView).maximized = true`, about 1920 x 888) and saves `ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotAsTexture()` after `WaitForEndOfFrame`; `NetDevHarness.Ui` does exactly this (`-executeMethod BuildingEscape.EditorTools.NetDevHarness.Ui`, no `-batchmode`; a run takes about a minute plus the compile). The Editor prints UIElements errors from its own windows on that desktop (`UIR.RenderChainCommand`), which are harmless. The same wrapper works for a Windows player build.
- **Gotchas**: Unity's batch runs empty `Library/PackageCache` of a scratch copy; refill it from a working project's `Library/PackageCache` before every launch. The Burst / `ILSupport.dll` errors in those logs are that hollow cache and are harmless. `Remove-Item` may be blocked by a hook in the agent's shell. Keep disk space free (each project copy is about 2 GB; the scratch drive had 15 GB left). `EditorApplication.delayCall` does not run in a batch `-quit` run: the catalog builders were run through `NetDevPewSetup.FillCatalogs`.
- Known failing lines: none since the night of 2026-10-16; 8.7 explains the 24 that used to fail.

### 8.7 Test failures seen in the last regression run (explained and fixed 2026-10-16, night)

**Result: all 24 lines that used to fail were tests that had gone stale or had gone around the game's own code. None was a fault in the game.** They are fixed in the tests (the game gained one Editor-only key hook, below), and `Doors` (76 checks), `Outpost` (73) and `Sneak` (46) end with 0 FAIL. Earlier sections that speak of "the known baseline" or "the known failures" (2.15, 2.16, 2.17, 8.5) mean these lines. History: they were first triaged on 2026-10-16 (none was caused by the pew work: the same lines on the tree without it), and explained the same night by a session that was asked "stale test or real fault?": the code behind every line was read, the test was changed, the scenario was run again, and the lock hack was played over the network as well (`OutpostNet`, below).

| Scenario | Lines that failed | Why (all stale or bypassing tests) | What was done |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Outpost` (19) | the four "hack:" lines "a wrong key sets the alarm off", "the alarm calls backup", "two officers were sent", "the lock is jammed while the alarm sounds"; the six "panel:" lines (the siren has stopped but the backup is still coming / it offers to shut the alarm off / the radios are jammed / an officer who spots somebody cannot call backup / pressing it again changes nothing / no new officers came); eight more "hack:" lines "a second hack can be tried after the alarm", "four right keys in time win (phase Lost, 0 keys)", "the lock is open and the player is free", "the door slides open and stays open", "the keypad can not be worked any more", "the network state says unlocked", "afterwards it is an ordinary door", "...and opens again"; "note: reading it brings the note screen up" | **The old scenario went around the hack panel.** A batch run has no keyboard, so it pressed the keys by calling `HackSession.Press` itself. `HackPanel.Update` only reports a hack that it sees come about in its own `Update` (`if (session.Finished) return;` at the top; `End` -> `OutpostController.SubmitHack` is the only way a result reaches the controller), so a session finished from outside was never reported: no alarm, no backup call, no unlock (the first four lines; with no alarm the six panel lines had nothing to switch off, and the scenario's own `CallBackup` then made the "5 -> 7" officers), and the Hack screen stayed up (`OutpostUi.Current` stuck on `Hack`). That refused every later `HackPanel.Begin` (it checks `OutpostUi.Open`), so the scenario kept reading the first, finished session (the "second hack" and "four right keys" lines, "phase Lost, 0 keys", and every "lock is open" line), and `IntelScreens.ShowNote` refuses while a screen is up (the note line). **It was not a timing problem** ("the keys are never pressed in time" in 2.15 was a wrong guess: no key was ever late). In a real game nothing finishes the session from outside, so a player never meets this. | `HackPanel.TestKeyDown` (Editor only, like `WobblyPlayer.testMove`): while it is set the panel asks it instead of `Input.GetKeyDown`. The scenario now plays the hack the way a player does: the crosshair on the locked door / the keypad (the game's own `Crosshair.Resolve`), E (`OutpostController.UseLockedDoor` / `KeypadProp.Use`, which is what the E handlers call), then W A S D and Esc through the panel's own `Update` (`Tap`). New checks: the crosshair on the door and on the keypad targets them; E on the door starts the hack; Esc gives it up with no alarm; a key pressed before the first prompt is ignored; "two officers" now really checks for two (5 -> 7). 73 checks, 0 FAIL. |
| `Sneak` (3, and 2 more that a crash hid) | "dock: the vendor has a chat bubble", "dock: walking up to him opens the bubble"; "dock: a hit takes health off the dummy (100 -> 100)"; hidden until a run with graphics: "dock: it goes down after enough hits (30 hits)", "dock: it heals when left alone (100 -> 100)" | The two bubble lines compared the vendor's line with the old placeholder "Shocked you made it back"; since the vendor's greeting was changed to the requested line (section 4) the line is `CustodyRules.GreetingLine` ("Glad you made it..."); the bubble itself was fine (the log said `visible True`). The three dummy lines: a swing is aimed by the crosshair (`Crosshair.SwingForward`: the camera's aim, not the way the body faces; section 2.9), the scenario only turned the body and the camera looked somewhere else in a batch run, so the swing missed (100 -> 100, and nothing to knock down or heal). The two hidden lines: in a `-nographics` run Unity crashed (access violation) in the first `ShotCam` (the picture of the dummy, `NetDevSneak.cs`), so everything after it, the crouch and voice checks too, never ran. | The scenario compares with `CustodyRules.GreetingLine` (the test holds no game text). The swing looks at the dummy like a player's camera does (`AimCamera`, as `Pew` / `Demo` do) and a new check proves the other half: with the camera looking away the swing misses. `ShotCam` (shared, `NetDevHarness.cs`) skips the picture when there is no graphics device ("SKIP picture ..." line), so `Sneak` runs to the end in both modes: 46 checks, 0 FAIL, with and without graphics (the pictures need graphics). |
| `Doors` (2) | "doors: one door under every doorway beam (114 doors, 113 beams)"; "aim: pointing at a door from far away targets nothing and says too far" | The beam count only knew `DoorHeader`; the guard outpost's locked front door has its own beam, `LockHeader` (wall kind `lockheader`, `FloorGenerator.Interior.cs`), and `BuildDoors` makes its door too: 113 + 1 (the older "125 doors, 124 beams" was the same). The aim line stood 5.5 m from the door, but the aim ray ends `reach + 1.2 m` past the player (`AimMath.EndT`, pinned by a pure check) = 3.4 m, so "too far" can only be told for a door between 2.2 m (the reach) and 3.4 m; at 5.5 m the ray never meets the door (Kind None, TooFar False), which is the design. | The count adds `LockHeader` (114 doors, 114 beams). The "too far" line stands 3.0 m from the door (TooFar True), and a second line stands 5.5 m away and expects nothing targeted. 76 checks, 0 FAIL. (A grey reticle from farther than 3.4 m would need a larger margin in `AimMath.EndT`: the owner's call.) |

**The demo's lock, played the way a player does it over the network (`OutpostNet`, new, `NetDevOutpostNet.cs`).** The pew lies inside floor 4's locked outpost, so the demo needs a player to hack that lock, usually the client. `runmp.ps1 -Method OutpostNet -Roles host,c1 -Players 2 -RunId on1` (host 8 checks, client 14, 0 FAIL): both machines go to floor 4 (`OutpostLoot.PewFloor`); the client stands at the keypad, the crosshair on it (`Crosshair.Resolve`), E, a wrong key through the panel's `Update` -> the **host** sets the alarm off, jams the lock and calls backup (8 -> 10 officers), and the client sees the siren and the jammed lock only through the host's state (`vOutpost`); after the 14 s the client hacks again with four right keys -> the host opens the lock and the door, the client sees the same (door open, keypad dead), goes in and takes the pew (the host sees it in his pack and in his hand). That is the whole `RunSync.RequestHack` / `HostHack` / `NetApply` path.

**What is proven and what is not.** Proven (batch Play Mode, offline and two processes): the crosshair reaches the keypad and the door; E starts the hack; W/A/S/D/Esc handling, the 0.6 s intro, the 1 s window, wrong key, too slow, Esc, stepping away, the alarm, backup, the jam, the panel inside, the win, the door, the host's verdict on both machines, the pew. **Not covered** (no keyboard, no screen in a batch run): the operating system's reading of the keys (`Input.GetKeyDown` for W A S D and Esc in `HackPanel`, for E in `Crosshair` and `DoorManager`), what the panel looks like (IMGUI is not drawn in batch mode; a hidden-desktop picture run like `Ui` would show it), and the feel of the 1 s window for a person. The owner should hack the floor 4 outpost once by hand (2.11 "Check first in Unity" 1-3, 2.17 "Check first" 2). Design note for the owner: a failed hack calls backup (two officers, armed on floor 4, and one more every 30 s), so the demo's friends will meet armed officers if they miss a key.

**Why the three scenarios kept failing unnoticed:** they were run in `-nographics` batch mode, where `Sneak` crashed before its last 20 checks, and the first triage compared trees (with and without the pew) rather than reading the lines. Run `Sneak` with graphics occasionally (`regress2.ps1 ... Sneak:gfx`) for the pictures; run `Outpost` and `OutpostNet` after any change to `HackPanel`, `OutpostController`, `RunSync.Outpost.cs` or `Crosshair`.

A new agent or developer: find out which of them still exists (the Claude app lists them); if both are gone, this section, 8.4 and 8.9 hold everything. **Update (2026-10-16, about 18:00): both sessions hit the usage limit at about 17:37 and agent 3 (session `e7511809-da6a-425a-b8c3-65a911ea9dca`, opened from the BuildingEscape folder) took over: it finished and applied task 4 (the guards' elevator and the stairwell doors) and task 5 (the compact HUD, pounds, the Keybinds screen), and then built and applied task 6 (the toon menus, 8.9). Both original sessions announced "auto-continue when the limits reset" (about 20:30): if they come back, tell them what is above before they edit anything.**

|---|---|---|

Rules both sessions follow:
- **Shared files are patched, never overwritten**: `DEVELOPER_NOTES.md`, the memory notes, `Editor/NetDev/NetDevTest.cs`, `Editor/NetDev/NetDevHarness.cs`. Re-read the real file right before an exact-match patch (the `Rep` helper of `patchlib.ps1` refuses to change anything that does not match exactly once). Copying a whole scratch file over a shared one would erase the other session's additions.
- Files only one task touches are applied by copying the verified file (CRLF, with its `.meta` for new scripts), after the backup of the replaced files (`real_backup_before_<name>` in the applying session's scratchpad), then compared by SHA-256 with the scratch tree.
- No git commands at all (the owner commits). After an apply the owner's Editor recompiles; they must stay out of Play Mode meanwhile.
- Which files belong to which task: task 4 (B): `Interior/FloorShape`, `OfficeLayout`, `OfficePlanner`, `OfficePlanner.Outpost`, `GuardAI`, `FloorGenerator`, `FloorGenerator.Outpost|AfterHours|Interior`, `NetDevOutpost`, checks in `NetDevTest`. Tasks 5 and 6 (A): everything under `Scripts/Hud/`, `HeistHud`, `BackupHud`, `CustodyHud`, `EscapeMenu`, `Net/LobbyMenu`, and one-line spacing edits in `Backpack`, `ElevatorExtraction`, `Net/Voice/VoiceChat`, plus `NetDevUi`, a new `NetDevMenus` scenario and a check in `NetDevTest`.

### 8.9 Tasks 5 and 6: a smaller HUD, the Keybinds screen, nicer lobby and Esc menus (plan, status, how to continue)

**The owner's words**, in the order they came: (5) "The UI should take up less of the screen, all of the controls can be put into a keybind screen that is accessed through the settings menu the bottom right pew(weapon UI) can be smaller", then "The middle UI where it says loading docks and what to do can also be smaller". (6) "Please also make the main lobby and escape menu nicer", then "The UI for them I mean". (7) "Make sure both this task and the background task(s) are noted for another agent or developer to pick up when we run out of credits. There should be a layout for what we've done and what we are working towards" (= 8.8, this section, 8.4 and the memory notes).

**What the screen looked like before** (pictures `ui_before/` in session A's scratchpad, from the `Ui` scenario at 1920 x 888): a 410 x 192 status block top left, a 350 x 136 pack card bottom left, a 350 x 136 pew ammunition panel bottom right, two rows of key hints along the whole bottom, and a 590 x 200 "LOADING DOCKS" banner with a long line of help under it in the middle top. The lobby menu and the Esc menu are plain Unity IMGUI boxes and buttons (placeholders).

**Delivery 1 (task 5): a smaller HUD and the Keybinds screen**

**Applied to the repo on 2026-10-16 (agent 3), after: compile 0 errors, 181 pure PASS (166 + 10 `HudUnitsTest` + 5 `KeybindTest`), pictures of the compact HUD (`Ui`, the hidden desktop) and of the Esc menu and the Keybinds screen (`Menus`) looked at.** What it is:
- `Scripts/Hud/HudWidgets.cs` rewritten with compact sizes (reference pixels at 1080 high; every widget still scales with `HudTheme.S`): status card 268 x 78 with the cash and a floor tag ("FLOOR 1/13", "DOCKS", "BOSS") on the first row and the quota bar under it; a row of small chips under the card (LOST, RUN TOTAL, and FREE / PINNED / BUSTED - "FREE 1" is no longer shown to a player on their own); pack card 236 wide, **its height follows what there is to say** (the weight bar always, a loot line only with loot, an "IN HAND" line only with something in hand; it grows upwards from the corner); pew ammunition panel 196 x 68 (name, count, one bullet per round; the bullets give way to the reload bar; no key caps); banner: title 32 px in a 48 px ribbon at the top edge (it was 58 px in 84) with 16 / 14 px lines under it; prompts drawn at 82 % and notices at 85 % of the size their callers ask for (`PromptShrink`, `NoticeShrink`; a second prompt is stacked `HudWidgets.PromptStep` below the first); smaller voice pill, test-mode tag, backup chip, pinned / busted panel (560 x 104) and tags; the big backup ring has radius 46 (was 62) and sits higher.
- **The key hints are gone from the HUD** (`HudWidgets.Controls` deleted, `HeistHud` no longer calls it; "TAB inventory" in the pack card and the "LMB fire / R reload" caps in the ammunition panel went too).
- `Scripts/Hud/HudTheme.cs`: `Plate(rect)` = the small card with thinner lines.
- **New `Scripts/Hud/KeybindScreen.cs`**: the Keybinds screen, three columns (MOVE, USE AND CARRY, PLACES AND TEAM) of key cap + short text, a tip line, a BACK button, Esc closes it; `KeybindScreen.Draw()` returns true when it was closed. It only lists the keys (**nothing can be rebound yet**; rebinding would mean replacing the `KeyCode` fields scattered over the scripts with one table: a possible follow-up). The list mirrors the real bindings (checked against the scripts on 2026-10-16: E, Tab, Q, LMB, R, H, J, F, G, M, T, Esc, F1, WASD, Shift, Ctrl / C, Space, Alt / V). **`HowToPlayContent.Controls` still lists "1 - 7  Buy from the vendor's shop": that is stale, the shop is clicked with the mouse.**
- Where it opens: lobby menu > SETTINGS > KEYBINDS (`Page.Keybinds` in `Net/LobbyMenu.cs`, drawn before the menu's own panel), and in a run Esc menu > Keybinds (`EscapeMenu`; Esc closes the screen first, then the menu).
- Callers adjusted: `HeistHud` (no hints, smaller end-screen button), `BackupHud` (ring and notices), `Backpack` / `ElevatorExtraction` (second prompt spacing), `Net/Voice/VoiceChat` (pill start).

**Pounds (the owner, later the same evening: "Can the weight also be in pounds not Kg please", "It should be Lbs", "All three letters lowercase")**: every weight the player reads is shown through `Scripts/WeightUnits.cs` (pure: `Lbs(kg)` = "8.8 lbs", `Num(kg)`, `PerLb`); the game still keeps kilograms internally (item weights, the pack capacity, `Backpack.capacity`), so no balance number changed (the 20 kg pack is 44.1 lbs). Changed: the pack card (`HudWidgets`), the pick-up prompts and notices, the inventory window and its "$ per lb" tip (`Backpack`), the shop (`Vendor`), the test menu capacity slider (`TestMode`). `NetDevTest.HudUnitsTest` checks the conversion and the lower-case unit; `NetDevTest.KeybindTest` checks the Keybinds list. The `Menus` picture scenario (`NetDevHarness.Menus`, hidden desktop, `NetDevMenus.cs`) opens the Esc menu and the Keybinds screen in a run. Still to do: nothing for delivery 1 except, if the owner asks, rebinding (see above).

**Delivery 2 (task 6): the lobby menu and the Esc menu in the toon style**

**The first plan** (the kit that was built is simpler, see "As built" below): a small widget kit `Scripts/Hud/MenuKit.cs` on top of `HudTheme` for the menus' scaled GUILayout space: `HudTheme.ScaleOverride` (so the kit draws in layout units while `GUI.matrix` does the scaling), a tall panel look (`HudTheme.Sheet`), title ribbon (tilted, pops when the page changes), section chips, big toon buttons (hover grows, press sinks, a colour per meaning), toggle, slider (own drag handling with `GUIUtility.hotControl`), selection pills, colour swatches, text field on an inset plate, list rows, a scroll view with a toon scroll bar (a tiny generated texture skin). Then rewrite `Net/LobbyMenu.cs` page by page with it (Main: title, Steam status chip, PLAY green / CUSTOMIZE blue / HOW TO PLAY gold / SETTINGS lavender / QUIT red; Play; Browse; the party lobby with a lobby-code chip, roster rows with ready chips and a big START RUN; Customize; Settings with a KEYBINDS button; How to play with key caps; Invite with status dots) and `EscapeMenu.OnGUI` (centred card: ribbon "MENU", two sliders, RESUME green, KEYBINDS blue, LEAVE RUN orange, EXIT GAME red). Keep every call into `NetSession` / `LobbyStage` / `VoiceChat` exactly as it is (the lobby menu is documented as replaceable without touching networking). Pictures: a `Menus` scenario in the Menu scene (and one in a run for the Esc menu) with `HudTheme`'s font, on the hidden desktop.

**As built and applied (2026-10-16 evening, agent 3).** `Scripts/Hud/MenuKit.cs` draws at absolute positions on top of `HudTheme` (no GUILayout, so no `ScaleOverride` was needed): `Sheet` (the tall card; its shaded band is only about 100 px deep), `Ribbon` (the title tab), `Button` (grows under the mouse, sinks when pressed; a disabled one is grey and never takes the mouse), `Pick` / `Choice` (pick-one pills, in a grid), `Swatch`, `Slider` / `SliderRow` (its own drag with `GUIUtility.hotControl`; the drag goes on when the mouse leaves the track), `ToggleRow` (an ON / OFF switch), `TextBox` (a cream plate with Unity's `GUI.TextField` on it under an empty `GUIStyle`: a copy of the skin's style brings its own dark background textures), `Scroll` (the wheel and a slim bar; a widget scrolled out of view cannot be clicked, `MenuKit.clip`), `Flow` / `Cell` (layout), `Wrapped` / `WrappedHeight` / `Fit` (wrapped text; a name that does not fit is cut with "..." instead of shrinking to a speck), `Header`, `StatusPill`, `Notice`, `Plate`, `Dim`. **Every widget takes its control id once per event, so call them on every event** (never skip one because the mouse is elsewhere), and they draw only on Repaint. `HudTheme.TextFont` / `TextStyle` give the HUD font to Unity's own text drawing; `HudAnim.Id.Esc` is the Esc card's pop.
- **`EscapeMenu.OnGUI`**: the screen dimmed, a card with the ribbon MENU, MASTER VOLUME and MUSIC VOLUME sliders with value chips, RESUME (green), KEYBINDS (blue), LEAVE RUN (orange, only in a networked run), EXIT GAME (red) and a note that the game keeps running. Esc behaves as before.
- **`Net/LobbyMenu.cs`**: one card on the left (`PanelW` 470 reference pixels; `LobbyStage.PanelPixels` follows it), **as tall as its page** (eased, up to the height of the screen); a page that does not fit scrolls; the button that leaves a page (QUIT GAME / BACK / DONE / LEAVE LOBBY / CANCEL) is pinned to the card's bottom edge. Main: the red OVERTIME ESCAPE logo plate, a Steam pill, PLAY (green), CUSTOMIZE (blue), HOW TO PLAY (orange), SETTINGS (violet). Play: HOST A LOBBY (PUBLIC / CODE ONLY / FRIENDS ONLY), JOIN (browse, a code box and JOIN BY CODE), SOLO, LOCAL TEST (host, an IP box and join). Browse: REFRESH and a row per lobby (name, players chip, OTHER VERSION chip, JOIN). The party lobby: the lobby code with a COPY button, the roster with HOST / YOU / READY chips, INVITE FRIENDS, CUSTOMIZE, START RUN (green, grey while it waits) or I AM READY, LEAVE LOBBY. Customize: CHARACTER pills, COLOUR swatches, HUE / SATURATION / BRIGHTNESS, FACE, HAT, BUILD sliders, RANDOMIZE / RESET, DONE. Settings: VOLUME, MOUSE SENSITIVITY, VOICE CHAT pills, VOICES, a FULLSCREEN switch, KEYBINDS. How to play: the sections and the controls with key caps (the text is `HowToPlayContent`, untouched; its stale "1 - 7" line is still there). Invite: a row per friend with a status light and INVITE. The session's status message ("Creating lobby...", why a join failed) shows under the title. **Esc steps back** out of Play, Browse, Settings, How to play and Invite (not Customize: DONE keeps the look). Every call into `NetSession`, `LobbyStage`, `VoiceChat`, `PlayerAppearance` and `FriendsService` is the one the old menu made.
- **Tests**: `NetDevHarness.Menus` (the Esc menu and the Keybinds screen in a run), the new `NetDevHarness.MenusLobby` (Boot, then the Menu scene: every page, made-up lobbies and friends, a local lobby, the status line, and Main / Customize again at 1280 x 720, 960 x 540 and 3840 x 2160 through the Game view's `SetCustomResolution`; the scenarios put the Game view back to Free Aspect, because a custom size would otherwise stay for every later picture) and `NetDevHarness.MenusRun` (a host-only networked run: the Esc menu with LEAVE RUN); pictures `%TEMP%\be_menu_*.png`; all on the hidden desktop, section 8.6; and **`NetDevMenuClicks.Run`** (`-executeMethod BuildingEscape.EditorTools.NetDevMenuClicks.Run`, no `-batchmode`, hidden desktop): an editor window with one of each widget is sent real IMGUI events with `EditorWindow.SendEvent` - clicks, a press released elsewhere, a disabled button, the slider's press / drag / release, a switch, typing into the text box and its length limit, the wheel, clipping of what is scrolled out of view, the bar's thumb, a button inside a scaled GUI matrix (the cards pop in with one) - 32 checks, all passing. Keys that type nothing (backspace, the arrows) are not delivered by `SendEvent`, so they are not tested. No pure tests were added (181 PASS, as before).
- **Not seen by the owner yet / not pictured**: the lobby code row and the READY chips of a Steam lobby (they need Steam), and the hover and pressed looks (pictures cannot show them; the click test shows they work). The card was pictured at 960 x 540, 1280 x 720, 1920 x 888 and 3840 x 2160 (below about 800 px of height it scrolls). Look at all of these in Unity first.

**Decisions made without asking (change them if the owner objects):** the HUD is smaller by design (about 60 % less area for the status block, 80 % less for the ammunition panel, the whole hint strip removed); a lone player does not see "FREE 1"; the Keybinds screen is read-only; the Esc menu got a Keybinds button because the HUD no longer shows any keys; the menus use the same toon look as the HUD (same palette, same font, same outline and shadow).

**How to continue if both sessions are gone:** (a) the private copy `BE_hud` may be gone with the scratchpad: re-create it from the repo (robocopy the project and a working `Library`, refill `Library/PackageCache` before each Unity run, section 8.6), then re-apply the files listed under "Built in BE_hud" from the repo once the owner has them (delivery 1 may already be applied: compare `Hud/HudWidgets.cs` in the repo with section 2.14 and 8.5 to see); (b) run the pure tests and the `Ui` picture scenario (8.6); (c) carry on with the lists above.

**3. The pistol work (B1) in detail**
- `Guns\GunRules.cs` `GunMagazine`: new `InfiniteReserve` (default TRUE, so the officers' guns and the old tests behave as before), `Reserve`, `SetReserve`, `AddReserve`, `CanReload`, `OutOfAmmo`; a reload now takes only the missing rounds out of the reserve; with an empty reserve `StartReload` is false and the trigger only clicks. `GunRules.SpareClips`.
- `Guns\RangedWeapon.cs`: pew `magazine = 8`, `spareClips = 2` (so 3 clips = 24 rounds), `guardHits = 4` (10-hit officer: 3 bullets; the 30-hit boss: 8). `NewMagazine` gives the player's gun a real reserve. `RefillAll` and the elevator refill are GONE (`TowerManager` no longer calls it): "more ammo should need to be purchased". The basement stays unlimited.
- `Guns\GuardGun.cs`: `MagazineSize = 10` constant, endless reserve (the officers are unchanged).
- `Guns\GunController.cs`: R / empty trigger on a dry gun says "Out of ammo - the dealer in the Loading Docks sells pistol clips" (every 3 s); a "SPARE n (k clips)" chip above the ammo panel (`DrawSpare`, orange at one clip, red at none); `Backpack` rows show "+n spare"; `TestMode` refill button refills the spare clips too.
- `Vendor.cs`: `PewPrice = 900`, `AmmoClipPrice = 120` (**my guesses, the owner should tune them**), `PewIndex 7`, `AmmoIndex 8` ("Pistol Clip", 8 rounds, only on offer when the pew is, i.e. after floor 3), `PewAfterFloor` (renamed from `FreePewAfterFloor`), `GunOf`, `GiveAmmo`; the ammo branch of `BuyFor` takes the money, then the host calls `RunSync.AnnounceAmmo` (new RPC `AmmoBoughtRpc`, added to `RunSync.Events.cs`) so every machine adds the rounds to the reserve of the buyer's pew (the magazine state is per machine: the shooter's machine owns it). No cap on spare clips (a cap would be checked against the host's stale copy of a client's gun).
